GRIN2D ↔ GPR6 LIKELY_SUBCOMPLEX low confidence ipTM 0.67 · pTM
Rationale
The pair is novel (no STRING record, no CellPhoneDB hit, 0 PubMed co-mentions) and compartment-compatible: both are brain-expressed plasma-membrane proteins. However, GRIN2D is an obligate subunit of heterotetrameric NMDA receptors (CellPhoneDB complex Glutamate_NMDA_1_2D_complex with GRIN1), and the fold used GRIN2D alone. The very large modeled interface (3225 A^2 buried, 639 contacts, interface pLDDT only 63.7, minimum separation 2.04 A) could sit on surfaces that GRIN1 or the second GRIN2 subunit occupies in the assembled receptor, so the dimer model cannot be accepted as it stands.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt annotates GRIN2D as a glycoprotein with 6 glycosylation sites (plus 4 disulfide bonds and 2 modified residues) and GPR6 as a glycoprotein and palmitoylated lipoprotein with 3 glycosylation sites and 1 lipidation site. The glycans on both proteins are extracellular and AF3 did not model them; if the 3225 A^2 interface involves the GRIN2D extracellular amino-terminal or ligand-binding domains, or the GPR6 N-terminus, glycans could sterically interfere. Whether any site lies on the interface is unknown without per-residue analysis. The PubMed PTM searches (6 hits for GRIN2D, 8 for GPR6) returned only expression and disease papers, none specifically about PTMs relevant to this interface.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for GRIN2D and GPR6 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for GRIN2D and GPR6. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction, and GPR6 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. The pair is not previously reported.
Other subunits
GRIN1
Follow-up (not yet executed): Folding was not available in this run. Recommended next steps: (1) Re-fold GPR6 with GRIN1 plus GRIN2D (ideally the 2:2 GRIN1/GRIN2D tetramer, or at least a GRIN1-GRIN2D heterodimer) and check whether the GPR6 interface persists on an exposed surface of the assembled receptor or overlaps the GRIN1 contact surface. (2) Inspect interface topology for a physically impossible mix of cytoplasmic and extracellular residues, and for the 6 GRIN2D and 3 GPR6 N-glycosylation sites at the contact surface. (3) Note the supplied specificity statistics are borderline: GPR6 was confident at 1 of 3 receptors tested, with p_twoway=0.053. (4) If the interface survives, test by co-immunoprecipitation or BRET in striatal neurons or co-transfected cells, checking whether constitutive GPR6 Gs/cAMP signalling modulates GluN2D-containing NMDAR currents.

EFNA3 ↔ GPR21 LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM low confidence ipTM 0.64 · pTM
Rationale
No database or literature records this pair (STRING not found, CellPhoneDB not found, PubMed co-occurrence 0), and the supplied breadth control is specific (EFNA3 confident against 1 of 9 receptors tested, p_twoway 0.0145), so it is novel and not an obviously promiscuous fold. However, both proteins are UniProt glycoproteins (EFNA3 P52797: 3 glycosylation sites plus 1 GPI lipidation; GPR21 Q99679: 2 glycosylation sites), and the only face of a 7-transmembrane receptor that a GPI-anchored extracellular ephrin can reach is its small extracellular surface, where GPCR glycosylation sites sit. I could not run a fold to check whether those sites lie on the modelled interface, so this is an unverified inference. The reported interface (buried surface 5310 Å2, 1124 contacts, minimum separation 1.88 Å, interface pLDDT 68.4, ipTM 0.64) also looks larger than that extracellular face could plausibly supply, which raises the possibility that the model packs ephrin against transmembrane or cytoplasmic regions it could not physically reach. Expression support is weak.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt annotates EFNA3 (P52797) with the keywords Glycoprotein, GPI-anchor and Lipoprotein, and counts 3 glycosylation sites, 1 lipidation site and 2 disulfide bonds. UniProt annotates GPR21 (Q99679) as a Glycoprotein with 2 glycosylation sites. The PubMed PTM search returned 11 hits for EFNA3 and 1 for GPR21, but the titles concern miR-210/EFNA3 signalling and plasmalogen signalling, not glycosylation, so they are uninformative. The concern is plausibly relevant because both glycosylated regions are extracellular, which is the only place the interaction could occur, and the GPR21 extracellular face is small. Whether any glycosylation site actually lies on the modelled interface was not verified, because no per-residue or fold tool was available in this run.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for EFNA3 and GPR21 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for the pair. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction, and GPR21 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. UniProt describes EFNA3 as a ligand for Eph receptor tyrosine kinases and GPR21 as an orphan receptor. The pair is novel with respect to these sources.
Other subunits
EPHA2;EPHA4
Follow-up (not yet executed): 1. Re-fold EFNA3 (mature ectodomain, without the signal peptide and GPI propeptide) with GPR21, and read the interface topology. If the interface touches GPR21 transmembrane-core or cytoplasmic residues, treat the prediction as an artifact. 2. If the interface is purely extracellular, check whether the GPR21 N-linked sites or the 3 EFNA3 glycosylation sites lie on it, and if so re-fold with the core glycans attached. 3. Inspect the 1.88 Å minimum separation for clashes. 4. Consider a competition fold with an Eph receptor ligand-binding domain (for example EPHA2 or EPHA4, which are EFNA3's canonical partners per UniProt, not CellPhoneDB complex members) to see whether the GPR21 contact uses the same ephrin loop. 5. Wet-lab test if the above pass: binding of EFNA3-Fc to cells expressing GPR21, with and without PNGase treatment.

IGF2 ↔ GPRC5C CONFIRMED_NOVEL VALIDATE low confidence ipTM 0.49 · pTM
Rationale
IGF2 is a secreted hormone (HPA: "Secreted to blood", function "Hormone") and GPRC5C is a 7-transmembrane orphan GPCR that HPA places at the plasma membrane, so a ligand-receptor geometry is compartmentally plausible. STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed co-occurrence all returned nothing for the pair, so it is novel. The structural support is borderline: ipTM is 0.49, just under the 0.5 convention, and interface pLDDT is 64.7, although interface PAE is 2.15 A and IGF2 was confident against only 1 of 17 receptors tested (p_twoway=0.020548). Treat this as a novel but unproven hypothesis, not a confident prediction.
PTM / glycosylation
GPRC5C (UniProt Q9NQ84) carries the keywords Glycoprotein and Phosphoprotein, with 1 annotated glycosylation site and 6 modified residues. The glycosylation site's position relative to the modeled interface is unknown; if it lies on the extracellular N-terminal region, a glycan there could sterically affect an extracellular IGF2 contact that AF3 modeled from bare sequence. The PubMed PTM search for GPRC5C returned 2 papers, neither specifically about glycosylation (angiotensin II-induced smooth muscle contraction; branched-chain amino acid metabolism in leukemogenesis). The uniprot_annotation lookup for IGF2 returned the wrong entry (Q6U949, "Putative insulin-like growth factor 2 antisense gene protein") rather than IGF2 itself, so no usable UniProt PTM data were obtained for IGF2. The PubMed PTM search for IGF2 returned 250 papers, but the top titles are about IGF2 signalling, not about IGF2 modification. This is an unresolved caveat, not evidence of an artifact.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for IGF2 and GPRC5C returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for IGF2 and GPRC5C. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction, and GPRC5C is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table at all. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. No hit in any of the three sources supports genuine novelty.
Other subunits
IGF1R;IGF2R;INSR
Follow-up (not yet executed): 1) Fold IGF2 (the true entry, P01344, mature peptide only, without the signal peptide and E-domain) with GPRC5C, and read the interface topology: the contacts must fall on GPRC5C's extracellular N-terminus, extracellular loops or transmembrane bundle, not its cytoplasmic tail. In the same fold, check whether the single GPRC5C N-glycosylation site lies on the interface; if it does, re-fold with the glycan attached. 2) Confirm that the chain-pair ipTM clears 0.5 with additional seeds, since the current 0.49 is below the convention. 3) Compare against IGF2 folded with its established receptors IGF1R, IGF2R and INSR as positive controls (these are listed as other_subunits for that benchmarking purpose only; no tool evidence indicates GPRC5C forms a complex with them). 4) Wet-lab: test IGF2 binding to GPRC5C-expressing cells by SPR or a cell-surface binding assay, with IGF1R/IGF2R blockade to exclude signal from the canonical receptors.

GNRH2 ↔ MRGPRX2 CONFIRMED_NOVEL VALIDATE medium confidence ipTM 0.65 · pTM
Rationale
GNRH2 (UniProt O43555, progonadoliberin-2) is HPA-classed "Predicted secreted proteins", secreted to blood, function "hormone"; MRGPRX2 (Q96LB1) is a plasma-membrane GPCR whose documented function is to be a promiscuous mast-cell receptor for BASIC secretagogue peptides (substance P, cortistatin-14, PAMP-12, LL-37, beta-defensins, dynorphins, PMX-53), so a cationic decapeptide hormone binding it is mechanistically the right class of interaction rather than an implausible one. No prior evidence exists for this specific pair: STRING returned found=false, CellPhoneDB returned no match, and pubmed_cooccurrence returned 0 hits for GNRH2 + MRGPRX2. The secreted-ligand/membrane-receptor geometry means same-cell coexpression is not required, so the ipTM=0.65 / interface PAE 2.2 A model is a genuinely novel, testable ligand hypothesis.
PTM / glycosylation
Neither protein carries a glycosylation or lipidation concern: GNRH2's ptm_topology_feature_counts show only 1 "Modified residue" and no Glycosylation; MRGPRX2 shows only 2 Disulfide bonds (AF3 handles cysteine proximity acceptably) and no Glycosylation. The real caveat is different and processing-related: GNRH2's UniProt keywords include "Amidation" and "Cleavage on pair of basic residues", i.e. the bioactive species is the C-terminally amidated decapeptide excised from the 86-residue precursor, whereas AF3 was given the bare unprocessed sequence. pubmed_ptm_glycosylation for GNRH2 returned 6 hits, all about GnRH receptor signalling in fish/pig rather than modification of the human peptide; the 41 MRGPRX2 hits are mast-cell degranulation papers unrelated to receptor PTMs.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
string_known_interaction: found=false (no channel, no textmining). cellphonedb_known_interaction: found=false, no matches. pubmed_cooccurrence: count=0, no hits. All three are negative, which is fairly strong evidence of genuine novelty for this pair.
Follow-up (not yet executed): I could not run fold_complex in this session (tool unavailable). Recommended fold: re-run AF3 with ONLY the mature amidated GnRH-II decapeptide (pGlu-His-Trp-Ser-His-Gly-Trp-Tyr-Pro-Gly-NH2), not the full precursor, against MRGPRX2, and read the structural_analysis to confirm the peptide sits inside the transmembrane bundle (the orthosteric site used by substance P and the other basic secretagogues) rather than draped on the extracellular loops. Bench test: MRGPRX2-transfected HEK293 or LAD2/human skin mast cell calcium-flux and beta-hexosaminidase release assays with synthetic GnRH-II, with a MRGPRX2-knockout or antagonist (e.g. QWF) control, and a counter-screen against GNRHR/GNRHR2 to show the response is MRGPRX2-specific.

A secretagogue is an agent that promotes the secretion of hormones, neurohormones, chemical neurotransmitters or other compounds synthesized and secreted by cells (PubMed:25517090). Neurogenic inflammation includes mast cell activation, recruitment of immune cells and release of inflammatory mediators, such as cytokines and chemokines (By similarity). The inflammatory response can then activate or sensitize nociceptors, promoting pain (By similarity). GHRL ↔ MRGPRX4 LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM medium confidence ipTM 0.65 · pTM
Rationale
GHRL (UniProt Q9UBU3) is a secreted hormone precursor whose documented binding mechanism is lipid-dependent: UniProt states "Octanoylated ghrelin-28 is the most commonly found ghrelin and octanoylation is essential for the activation of the growth hormone secretagogue receptor type 1 (GHSR)", with keyword "Lipoprotein" and 3 annotated Lipidation features — a modification AF3 does not represent in a bare-sequence fold, so the modeled GHRL surface is not the physiological, acylated ligand surface. MRGPRX4 (Q96LA9) is moreover not a peptide receptor at all but a G protein-coupled receptor for bile acids (deoxycholate, chenodeoxycholate, 3-sulfated bile acids), whose orthosteric pocket is built for a small sterol-like ligand, making a 3046 Å² peptide interface at ipTM 0.65 chemically off-mechanism. No prior evidence links the pair (STRING not found, CellPhoneDB not found, PubMed co-occurrence 0), so the pair is novel, but novelty here most likely reflects an unmodeled-PTM/wrong-ligand-class artifact rather than a real ligand-receptor hypothesis.
PTM / glycosylation
GHRL: UniProt keywords include "Lipoprotein" and "Amidation"; ptm_topology_feature_counts show Lipidation x3 and Modified residue x1. The Ser3 octanoylation is explicitly described as essential for receptor activation, i.e. the real binding mechanism is lipid-mediated and entirely absent from AF3's bare-sequence model. MRGPRX4: keyword "Glycoprotein" with 2 Glycosylation sites and 2 disulfide bonds; whether a glycan sits on the modeled interface could not be checked (fold_complex unavailable in this run). PubMed PTM searches were largely off-target (GHRL hits concern tau pathology, avian GH release, frailty biomarkers; MRGPRX4 hits concern phospho-drug pruritus and a phosphorylation switch for lipid GPCR activators).
PubMed co-occurrence
pubmed_cooccurrence(GHRL, MRGPRX4) = 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING: no association found for GHRL–MRGPRX4. CellPhoneDB: no curated interaction (MRGPRX4 is not even in the CellPhoneDB gene table). PubMed co-occurrence: 0 papers mentioning both genes. By the novelty criteria this pair is genuinely unreported.
Follow-up (not yet executed): Re-run the AF3 multimer with the mature acylated ghrelin peptide (Ser3 n-octanoyl) rather than the bare prepropeptide, and with MRGPRX4's two N-glycans represented, to see whether the interface survives and whether the contacts fall inside the transmembrane bundle (the bile-acid orthosteric site) or merely on extracellular loops — I could not run this fold in the current session. Wet-lab test would be a Gq/calcium-mobilisation or IP3 assay in MRGPRX4-expressing cells with acyl- and des-acyl-ghrelin, plus a competition assay against deoxycholate 3-sulfate; a GHSR-expressing line is the positive control.

FGA ↔ GPR101 LIKELY_SUBCOMPLEX low confidence ipTM 0.53 · pTM
Rationale
No database or literature records this pair (STRING not found, CellPhoneDB not found, PubMed 0 hits), and a secreted plasma protein meeting a cell-surface GPCR is topologically permissible. However, FGA does not circulate alone: CellPhoneDB lists it in the obligate fibrinogen complex with FGB and FGG, so a fold of the bare alpha chain exposes coiled-coil and disordered surface that is buried or differently arranged in the real molecule. The model is also marginal and not specific: ipTM is 0.53, interface pLDDT is 68.8, p_twoway is 0.051, and FGA scored as confident against 2 of the 3 receptors it was tested with.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt marks FGA (P02671) as a Glycoprotein and Phosphoprotein with 4 glycosylation sites, 16 modified residues and 8 disulfide bonds; it is also cleaved by thrombin and cross-linked through isopeptide bonds. UniProt marks GPR101 (Q96P66) as a Glycoprotein with 2 glycosylation sites and 1 disulfide bond; the 2 sites were not mapped to a domain. The PubMed PTM searches (22 hits for FGA, 1 for GPR101) returned nothing specific to this interface. Glycans on either protein could sit on an extracellular contact surface, but whether any glycosylation site lies on the modeled interface was not determined, so this is a secondary caveat and not the basis for the verdict.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for FGA and GPR101 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for FGA and GPR101. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction, and GPR101 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. The pair is novel with respect to these sources. GPR101 is annotated by UniProt as an orphan receptor.
Other subunits
FGB;FGG
Follow-up (not yet executed): No fold was run for this triage. Recommended: (1) re-fold GPR101 with FGA plus FGB plus FGG (one alpha/beta/gamma half-molecule) and see whether the GPR101 interface persists at chain-pair ipTM of at least 0.5 on a surface that is exposed in assembled fibrinogen. (2) Check that the interface residues on GPR101 are extracellular or transmembrane-boundary only, not cytoplasmic. (3) Check whether any of the 4 FGA or 2 GPR101 glycosylation sites lie on the interface. (4) Inspect the 1.85 Å minimum separation for clashes and check whether the 3470 Å2 interface is driven by FGA's disordered alpha-C region. (5) Counter-screen FGA against more unrelated GPCRs, since 2 of 3 receptors tested were already confident, which suggests non-specific docking.

FGFR1 ↔ GPR50 INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE low confidence ipTM 0.38 · pTM
Rationale
The pair is unreported (STRING found nothing, CellPhoneDB found nothing, PubMed co-occurrence count 0) and biologically possible: both are membrane proteins, both HPA-annotated at the primary cilium, and FGFR1 is broadly expressed so it should be present in the pituitary lactotrophs where GPR50 is enriched. The structural support is contradictory, though: ipTM is 0.38, below the 0.5 convention for a predicted interaction, and p_twoway is 0.058, while interface PAE is 2.42 A and only 1 of 4 partner receptors tested passed the PAE gate. The side of the membrane the interface sits on could not be checked without a fold, and FGFR1 has 8 glycosylation sites, so neither a novel-interaction call nor an artifact call is supported.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt lists FGFR1 as a Glycoprotein with 8 glycosylation sites (on its extracellular immunoglobulin domains), plus the keywords Heparin-binding and Phosphoprotein and 7 modified residues. GPR50 has no glycosylation or lipidation features in UniProt (1 disulfide bond only). The PubMed PTM search returned 674 hits for FGFR1 and 4 for GPR50, but the titles shown were not specific to glycosylation at any interface. Whether a glycosylation site lies on the modeled interface is unknown, because the interface residues and their topology were not available. If the contact involves the FGFR1 ectodomain, glycan occlusion is a real concern; if it is transmembrane or cytoplasmic, it is not.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for FGFR1 and GPR50 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for FGFR1 and GPR50. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction, and GPR50 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. The pair is novel as far as these sources show. As precedent only, UniProt (Q13585) describes GPR50 activating another single-pass receptor kinase, TGFBR1, by interfering with FKBP1A binding; this is not evidence for FGFR1.
Other subunits
KL
Follow-up (not yet executed): Re-fold FGFR1 with GPR50 and read the interface topology to establish whether the contact is ectodomain, transmembrane or cytoplasmic, and whether any of the 8 FGFR1 glycosylation sites sit on it; this could not be run here. The very large interface (buried surface area 7088 A^2, 1306 contacts, interface pLDDT 64.7, minimum separation 2.16 A) across a 1439-token complex should be inspected in 3D for extended low-confidence packing rather than a discrete interface, and for any interface touching both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues at once (physically impossible; AF3 models no bilayer). Consider truncating the long GPR50 C-terminal tail and folding domain-level constructs to see whether ipTM rises above 0.5. CellPhoneDB lists FGFR1 in a Klotho_FGFR1 complex with KL, and FGFR1 normally signals as an FGF/heparan-sulfate-bound dimer, so a fold with a co-receptor or ligand is a reasonable secondary test. If the fold holds up, test by co-immunoprecipitation or BRET in a pituitary lactotroph-derived or hypothalamic cell line.

WNT3 ↔ GPR101 LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM low confidence ipTM 0.55 · pTM
Rationale
WNT3 (secreted) and the orphan GPCR GPR101 (cell membrane) are a compartment-plausible ligand/receptor pair with no prior record in STRING, CellPhoneDB or PubMed (0 co-mentions), so the pair is novel. However, UniProt annotates WNT3 (P56703) as a Lipoprotein and Glycoprotein (1 lipidation site, 2 glycosylation sites) and GPR101 (Q96P66) as a Glycoprotein (2 glycosylation sites), none of which AF3's bare-sequence model represents; Wnt receptor engagement is generally understood (background knowledge, not from the tools) to depend on the lipid modification. The fold itself is only marginal (ipTM 0.55, interface pLDDT 67.7, p_twoway 0.053), and I could not check whether any modification site actually sits on the modeled interface, so the artifact call is a caution, not a demonstrated result.
PTM / glycosylation
WNT3 (UniProt P56703): keywords Lipoprotein and Glycoprotein; feature counts are 1 lipidation, 2 glycosylation and 11 disulfide bonds. The PubMed PTM search returned 52 hits, including PMID 38994173, "N-glycosylation of Wnt3 regulates the progression of hepatocellular carcinoma by affecting Wnt/β-catenin signal pathway". GPR101 (UniProt Q96P66): keyword Glycoprotein; 2 glycosylation sites and 1 disulfide bond; the PubMed PTM search returned 1 hit, which is not about GPR101 glycosylation. The WNT3 lipid and the glycans on both proteins are absent from the AF3 model and are plausibly relevant to an extracellular ligand/receptor interface, but whether any of these sites lies on the modeled interface could not be checked because no fold tool was available in this run.
PubMed co-occurrence
pubmed_cooccurrence returned 0 hits for WNT3 and GPR101.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING: no association found for WNT3 and GPR101. CellPhoneDB: no curated interaction, and GPR101 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table. PubMed co-occurrence: 0 papers. The pair is unreported. UniProt describes WNT3 as a ligand for frizzled-family seven-transmembrane receptors and GPR101 as an orphan receptor.
Other subunits
FZD7;LRP6
Follow-up (not yet executed): 1) Not run here (fold tools unavailable): re-fold WNT3 with GPR101 and read the structural analysis for (a) whether the WNT3 lipidation site, the 2 WNT3 glycosylation sites or the 2 GPR101 glycosylation sites sit on the interface, and (b) whether the GPR101 contact residues are Extracellular/Transmembrane rather than Cytoplasmic. If an N-glycosylation site is on the interface, re-fold with the glycan attached. 2) FZD7 and LRP6 in other_subunits are not tool-derived (CellPhoneDB returned no complexes for either protein); they are suggested from general knowledge of canonical Wnt receptors as a comparison fold, to see whether WNT3 uses the same surface on GPR101 as it would on a Frizzled receptor. 3) The supplied breadth data show WNT3 was confident against only 1 of 5 receptors tested, which argues against a generically sticky ligand; a wet-lab test would be binding or signalling of lipidated WNT3 conditioned medium on GPR101-expressing cells.

CADM4 ↔ GPR37 CONFIRMED_NOVEL VALIDATE low confidence ipTM 0.47 · pTM
Rationale
CADM4 and GPR37 are both membrane proteins enriched in brain and in oligodendrocytes (HPA), and UniProt independently ties both to oligodendrocyte differentiation and CNS myelination, so a cis interaction on the same cell is biologically plausible. STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed (0 papers) all return nothing for the pair, so it is novel. Confidence is low because the supplied ipTM of 0.47 is below the 0.5 convention for a predicted interaction (despite interface PAE 2.54 A and 1 of 8 partner receptors passing the gate), interface pLDDT is only 65.3, and both ectodomains carry 3 N-glycosylation sites each whose position relative to the interface could not be checked without a fold.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt: CADM4 (Q8NFZ8) is a glycoprotein with 3 glycosylation sites, 1 lipidation (palmitoylation) site and 3 disulfide bonds; its function text says the palmitoylated form is required for oligodendrocyte maturation and myelination via association with CADM2/3 and MAG. GPR37 (O15354) is a glycoprotein with 3 glycosylation sites and 1 disulfide bond. The PubMed PTM searches returned 3 hits for CADM4 and 10 for GPR37, but the titles shown do not concern glycosylation of either protein's interface (the GPR37 top hit is about glycosylation of its ligand prosaposin). Glycans on either ectodomain could occlude the modeled contact surface if the interface is extracellular; this could not be checked per residue because no fold was available in this run. This is a real but unresolved caveat, not a demonstrated artifact.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers mentioning CADM4 and GPR37 together.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING: no association found. CellPhoneDB: no curated interaction for the pair. PubMed co-occurrence: 0 papers. No evidence anywhere that this pair is already reported.
Other subunits
CADM2;CADM3;MAG
Follow-up (not yet executed): 1) Re-fold CADM4 + GPR37 and read the interface topology: confirm the contacts are extracellular/transmembrane on both chains and not a mix of cytoplasmic and extracellular residues, and check whether any of the 3 N-glycosylation sites on each protein lies on the interface; if one does, re-fold with the glycan attached. The very large buried area (3453 A2, 631 contacts) at interface pLDDT 65.3 and a minimum separation of 1.77 A suggests extensive transmembrane-helix packing or a loosely packed model, which should be inspected in 3D. 2) The ipTM of 0.47 is below 0.5, so treat the model as a hypothesis only. 3) Wet-lab test: co-immunoprecipitation or proximity ligation of CADM4 and GPR37 in oligodendrocyte cultures or co-transfected cells, ideally also with CADM4 palmitoylation-deficient mutant. 4) CellPhoneDB lists no complexes for either protein; the suggested other subunits (CADM2, CADM3, MAG) come only from the UniProt function text for CADM4, and a CORUM check is still needed.

LGALS3 ↔ GPR39 LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM medium confidence ipTM 0.53 · pTM
Rationale
The pair is compartmentally plausible and unreported: galectin-3 is secreted (HPA "Secreted to blood") and GPR39 is a plasma-membrane GPCR, with no STRING or CellPhoneDB record and only one incidental PubMed co-mention. However, UniProt describes LGALS3 (P17931) as a "Galactose-specific lectin" and GPR39 (O43194) as a glycoprotein with 3 annotated glycosylation sites, so any real galectin-3 binding to GPR39 would be expected to go through GPR39's N-glycans, which the bare-sequence AF3 model does not contain. The AF3 protein-protein interface (ipTM 0.53, interface pLDDT 66.8, interface PAE 2.59 A, confident for 1 of 5 partner receptors tested) is borderline in ipTM and cannot represent that mechanism, so the modeled contact is more likely an artifact than a novel protein-protein interface.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt P17931 (LGALS3) function text begins "Galactose-specific lectin which binds IgE" and carries the keyword "Lectin"; its own feature counts show 4 modified residues and 1 disulfide bond and no glycosylation. UniProt O43194 (GPR39) carries the keyword "Glycoprotein" with 3 annotated Glycosylation features, plus 2 disulfide bonds and 1 modified residue. Galectin-3 binding to cell-surface receptors is canonically mediated by its carbohydrate-recognition domain binding beta-galactoside-containing glycans on the receptor, which AF3 does not model from bare sequence. The PubMed PTM searches returned 39 hits for LGALS3 and 25 for GPR39, but the titles shown are general disease/signalling papers and do not specifically address glycosylation of either protein, so the UniProt annotations are the basis for this caveat. Whether the 3 GPR39 glycosylation sites fall on the modeled interface is not known.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence count is 1: PMID 40616194, "DNA methylation and hydroxymethylation combined with transcriptional profiling identify key regulators of hyperoxia-induced bronchopulmonary dysplasia" (Clin Epigenetics, 2025). This looks like an unrelated co-mention in an epigenomic/transcriptomic screen, not evidence of binding.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for LGALS3-GPR39 (found: false). CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction (found: false, no matches). PubMed co-occurrence returned 1 hit (PMID 40616194, a DNA methylation/hydroxymethylation and transcriptional profiling study of hyperoxia-induced bronchopulmonary dysplasia), which appears to co-mention both genes in an omics gene list and is not a report of a direct interaction. The pair is therefore not already known.
Other subunits
HTR1A;GALR1
Follow-up (not yet executed): Folding was not available in this run. Recommended: (1) re-fold LGALS3 with GPR39 and read the interface topology to establish whether the modeled contact is on GPR39's extracellular face, its cytoplasmic face, or implausibly both, and whether any of the 3 UniProt N-glycosylation sites of GPR39 sit on the interface; (2) if a glycosylation site is on the interface, re-fold with the core N-glycan covalently attached at the true UniProt site and see whether the interface survives; (3) wet-lab test of glycan dependence, for example galectin-3 binding to GPR39-expressing cells with and without lactose competition or PNGase F treatment, and with a carbohydrate-recognition-domain mutant of galectin-3. Rationale

The AF3 signal is borderline (ipTM 0.51, interface pLDDT 65.4), and the 3488 A^2 interface is unusually large for a weak prediction. STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed all return nothing for the pair, so nothing offsets these problems. GFRA3 is a GPI-anchored protein and GPR37L1 is a GPCR, so both are membrane proteins. Without co-expression, though, this is no reason to expect them to meet. The glycosylation sites on both ectodomains are a separate unresolved caveat, but they don't rescue the pair.

PubMed co-occurrence

PTN ↔ GPR22 LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM low confidence ipTM 0.45 · pTM
Rationale
PTN (secreted growth factor) and the orphan GPCR GPR22 are compartment-compatible, both brain-enhanced, and have no prior record as a pair (STRING not found, CellPhoneDB not found, PubMed 0 hits), so the pair is genuinely novel. However, UniProt describes PTN's documented receptor-binding mechanism as glycan-mediated (it binds cell-surface proteoglycan receptors via their chondroitin sulfate groups and is heparin-binding), and GPR22 is a glycoprotein with 2 annotated glycosylation sites; AF3's bare-sequence model represents neither. The supplied ipTM of 0.45 is also below the 0.5 convention for a predicted interaction, even though the interface PAE is 2.67 A, the interface pLDDT is 70.9, and only 1 of 4 receptors tested against PTN passed the breadth gate. I could not check whether the GPR22 glycosylation sites sit on the modeled interface, so this is a caveat call rather than a demonstrated artifact; the alternative reading is a low-confidence novel ligand hypothesis for an orphan receptor.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt PTN (P21246): the function text states it binds cell-surface proteoglycan receptors via their chondroitin sulfate groups, and it carries the Heparin-binding keyword; this is a glycan-mediated mechanism that AF3 cannot represent. PTN itself has 5 disulfide bonds and no annotated glycosylation, which is not a concern. UniProt GPR22 (Q99680): Glycoprotein keyword with 2 annotated glycosylation sites; these are not modeled by AF3, and their position relative to the modeled interface was not checked. The PubMed PTM searches were uninformative: 84 hits for PTN and 2 for GPR22, and the titles returned are not about PTMs of these proteins. PTN is a strongly basic heparin-binding protein, so a large modeled interface (buried surface area 2427 A^2, 539 contacts) could reflect non-specific electrostatic packing; this is my inference, not a tool result. The supplied minimum separation of 1.82 A is short and should be checked for a clash.
PubMed co-occurrence
pubmed_cooccurrence returned 0 hits for PTN and GPR22.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING: no association found for PTN-GPR22. CellPhoneDB: no curated interaction found (GPR22 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table). PubMed co-occurrence: 0 papers. No evidence anywhere that this pair is already known. UniProt lists PTN's established receptors as PTPRZ1, ALK, SDC3, GPC2 and integrin alpha-V/beta-3, none of which is a GPCR; GPR22 is described as an orphan receptor signalling through G(i)/G(o).
Other subunits
PTPRZ1
Follow-up (not yet executed): fold_complex was not available in this run. Recommended next steps: (1) re-fold PTN with GPR22 and read the interface topology and PTM-at-interface output to confirm that the contacts are on GPR22's extracellular face and whether either of its 2 glycosylation sites lies on the interface; (2) if a site is on the interface, re-fold with the core N-glycan attached; (3) inspect the 1.82 A minimum separation for a steric clash; (4) because the ipTM of 0.45 is below 0.5, do not treat this as a positive without orthogonal support, for example a Gi/Go signalling assay (cAMP inhibition) in GPR22-expressing cells treated with recombinant PTN, with and without chondroitinase or heparin competition to test glycan dependence. PTPRZ1 is listed under other subunits only as PTN's canonical receptor from UniProt, not as a demonstrated member of a complex with GPR22; no CORUM check was possible.

NMS ↔ GPR158 CONFIRMED_NOVEL VALIDATE low confidence ipTM 0.56 · pTM
Rationale
NMS is a secreted brain neuropeptide (HPA "Predicted secreted proteins", secretome_location "Secreted in brain", function "regulation of circadian rhythms through autocrine and/or paracrine actions") and GPR158 is a brain/retina-enriched plasma-membrane GPCR ("Metabotropic glycine receptor", Cell membrane/Postsynaptic cell membrane), so a secreted-ligand/membrane-receptor pairing is compartmentally coherent and both are brain-expressed. No prior evidence exists for this specific pair anywhere: STRING returns found=false, CellPhoneDB returns no match, and pubmed_cooccurrence returns 0 hits — genuine novelty. Confidence is low because the fold itself is marginal for a protein pair in the supporting sense (ipTM 0.56 with interface pLDDT only 56.0, i.e. a poorly-ordered interface over a very large 5202 Å² buried surface and min separation 2.41 Å), and because GPR158's known orthosteric mechanism is binding of the small amino acid glycine/taurine in its extracellular Cache domain, not a peptide ligand, while NMS's own documented receptors are NMUR1/NMUR2.
PTM / glycosylation
GPR158 (Q5T848) UniProt lists keyword "Glycoprotein" with 5 annotated Glycosylation sites plus 2 disulfide bonds, 6 modified residues and Ubl conjugation; the glycosylation is on the extracellular face where any NMS contact would have to occur, so unmodelled glycans could sterically occlude the predicted interface. NMS (Q5H8A3) carries keywords "Amidation" and "Cleavage on pair of basic residues" (1 modified residue): the bioactive species is a short processed, C-terminally amidated peptide, whereas AF3 was given the bare unprocessed precursor sequence — the 5202 Å² interface therefore may involve propeptide regions that do not exist in the mature secreted ligand. pubmed_ptm_glycosylation for GPR158 returned 3 hits, all GPR158 neuronal-signalling papers rather than PTM-specific ones; the 61 hits for "NMS" are keyword noise (ribosome mass spectrometry, PLK1 inhibitors) and not about neuromedin S.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
string_known_interaction: found=false (no channel evidence at all). cellphonedb_known_interaction: found=false, no matches. pubmed_cooccurrence: 0 papers mentioning NMS and GPR158 together. All three negative, which is fairly strong evidence of genuine novelty rather than a rediscovery.
Other subunits
RGS7;GNB5
Follow-up (not yet executed): fold_complex was not available in this run. Recommended folds: (1) re-run AF3 with the MATURE processed NMS peptide (post-cleavage, C-terminally amidated if representable) rather than the full precursor, since the current 1117-contact/5202 Å² interface at pLDDT 56 is likely dominated by disordered propeptide; (2) check whether the modeled contacts fall on GPR158's extracellular Cache/ectodomain versus its cytoplasmic RGS7-GNB5-binding surface — an interface touching cytoplasmic residues would be topologically impossible for a secreted peptide; (3) check whether any of GPR158's 5 UniProt N-glycosylation sites sit on the modeled interface, and if so re-fold with the core glycan attached. Bench-side, test NMS displacement of glycine at GPR158 and counter-screen NMS against NMUR1/NMUR2 as positive controls.

GP1BB ↔ GPR63 LIKELY_SUBCOMPLEX low confidence ipTM 0.58 · pTM
Rationale
No database or literature record of this pair exists (STRING not found, CellPhoneDB not found, 0 PubMed co-mentions), so it is novel. However, CellPhoneDB lists GP1BB as a subunit of the Glycoprotein_Ib_complex with GP1BA, and the modeled pair omits that partner, so the modest AF3 interface (ipTM 0.58, interface pLDDT 75.2) may sit on GP1BB surfaces that are occupied in the native complex. Both proteins are also N-glycosylated membrane proteins (UniProt: 1 site on GP1BB, 3 on GPR63), and I could not check whether those sites fall on the interface because no fold was available in this run.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt marks GP1BB as a Glycoprotein and Phosphoprotein with 1 glycosylation site, 5 disulfide bonds and 2 modified residues. UniProt marks GPR63 as a Glycoprotein with 3 glycosylation sites. The PubMed PTM search returned 3 hits for GP1BB, none specifically about its glycosylation at an interface, and 0 hits for GPR63. Whether any of these glycosylation sites lies on the AF3-modeled interface could not be determined without a fold, so glycan occlusion remains an open caveat rather than an established artifact.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for GP1BB and GPR63 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for GP1BB and GPR63. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction, and GPR63 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. There is no prior report of this pair.
Other subunits
GP1BA;GP9
Follow-up (not yet executed): Recommended fold, which could not be run here: GP1BB + GPR63 + GP1BA (optionally also GP9) to see whether the GPR63 interface survives when GP1BB's native partners are present. GP1BA comes from CellPhoneDB; GP9 is my own addition from general knowledge of the GPIb-IX complex and was not returned by any tool. Inspect the interface topology in that fold to confirm it does not mix cytoplasmic and extracellular residues, and check whether the GP1BB N-glycosylation site or the three GPR63 sites lie on the interface. The supplied specificity data cover only one partner receptor (1 tested, 1 confident; p_twoway = 0.05042), so GP1BB should also be folded against a few unrelated GPCRs as a counter-screen. Confirm GP1BB protein, not just RNA, in choroid plexus or neurons before any bench work. CORUM is not available, so wider complex membership is unchecked.

CEACAM1 ↔ MRGPRX3 LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM low confidence ipTM 0.59 · pTM
Rationale
No database or literature records this pair (STRING not found, CellPhoneDB not found, 0 PubMed co-mentions), so it is novel, and UniProt places both proteins at the cell membrane. However, CEACAM1 (P13688) carries 20 annotated N-glycosylation sites on its extracellular Ig domains, which AF3's bare-sequence model does not represent, and a 3024 A^2 / 524-contact interface with a 7-TM GPCR is likely to include glycan-shielded surface. The fold itself is only moderate (ipTM 0.59, interface pLDDT 69.4, p_twoway 0.049) and HPA shows no shared tissue or cell type. I could not check which residues form the interface, so the glycan concern is inferred, not demonstrated.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt annotates CEACAM1 (P13688) with the keyword Glycoprotein and 20 Glycosylation features, plus 3 disulfide bonds and 4 modified residues (Phosphoprotein); its documented function is homophilic adhesion through its extracellular Ig domains. The PubMed PTM/glycosylation search for CEACAM1 returned 112 papers, including "Conformational Preferences for N-Glycans at the Surface of CEACAM1-Ig1" (PMID 41416709) and N-glycoproteomic profiling studies, which confirms that the ectodomain is heavily N-glycosylated in vivo. MRGPRX3 (Q96LB0) has no glycosylation or lipidation features in UniProt and 0 PubMed PTM hits. Because the CEACAM1 ectodomain is the part available to contact a GPCR's extracellular face, and the modeled interface is large (buried surface area 3024 A^2, 524 contacts), glycans plausibly occlude part of it. This is not confirmed: no per-residue interface data was available, and if the contact is instead transmembrane helix packing, the glycan concern would not apply.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for CEACAM1 and MRGPRX3 returned a count of 0 with no hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for CEACAM1-MRGPRX3 (found: false). CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction (found: false, no matches), and MRGPRX3 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table at all. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. There is no prior record of this pair anywhere checked, so it is novel with respect to these sources.
Follow-up (not yet executed): Recommended fold, which could not be run in this session: re-fold CEACAM1 + MRGPRX3 with structural analysis to see whether the interface residues are on the CEACAM1 extracellular Ig domains, the transmembrane helix or the cytoplasmic tail, and whether any of the 20 UniProt N-glycosylation sites lie on the interface. If glycan sites are on the interface, re-fold with the core N-glycans attached at those sites and check whether interface confidence survives. Also inspect the 3D model for an interface that touches both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues at once, which would be physically impossible, and for generic single-pass TM helix packing against the 7-TM bundle, a common artifact when no lipid bilayer is modeled. Before any bench work, confirm coexpression in a relevant cell type (for example, dorsal root ganglion sensory neurons or skin for MRGPRX3 versus CEACAM1-positive epithelial or neutrophil populations). Note that MRGPRX3 is an orphan receptor described by UniProt as potently activated by enkephalins, which are small peptides and not Ig-domain adhesion proteins.

TNFRSF8 ↔ GPR65 CONFIRMED_NOVEL VALIDATE low confidence ipTM 0.66 · pTM
Rationale
No prior record of this pair exists: STRING found nothing, CellPhoneDB found nothing, and PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. Both are membrane receptors that can share a membrane (TNFRSF8/CD30 at the plasma membrane; GPR65 annotated by UniProt at the cell membrane and endosome), and the supplied fold is moderately confident and partner-specific (ipTM 0.66, interface PAE 2.95 A, only 1 of 4 partner receptors tested was confident, p_twoway 0.028). Confidence is low because same-cell coexpression is not demonstrated by HPA, both proteins are N-glycosylated on their extracellular regions (4 and 3 UniProt sites) with no check of whether those sites sit on the interface, and the interface pLDDT is only 68.8.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt marks both proteins as Glycoprotein: TNFRSF8 (P28908) has 4 annotated glycosylation sites, 14 disulfide bonds and 2 modified residues; GPR65 (Q8IYL9) has 3 annotated glycosylation sites and 2 disulfide bonds. The pubmed_ptm_glycosylation searches returned 4 hits for TNFRSF8 and 6 for GPR65, but the titles are general biology papers and none specifically addresses glycosylation of an interaction surface. Neither protein's function text describes a glycan-mediated binding mechanism (TNFRSF8 is the receptor for TNFSF8/CD30L; GPR65 is a proton-sensing GPCR). Whether any glycosylation site falls on the modeled interface is unknown because no fold was available in this run; with a buried surface area of 5561 A^2 and 1149 contacts, extracellular glycan sites being involved is a real possibility that remains unchecked.
PubMed co-occurrence
pubmed_cooccurrence returned a count of 0 papers mentioning TNFRSF8 and GPR65 together.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
string_known_interaction returned found=false. cellphonedb_known_interaction returned found=false with no matches (GPR65 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table, so that miss is uninformative for GPR65). pubmed_cooccurrence returned 0 hits. There is no prior report of this pair in any of the three sources.
Other subunits
TNFSF8
Follow-up (not yet executed): 1) Re-fold TNFRSF8 + GPR65 and read the interface topology: confirm the contacts are extracellular/transmembrane on both chains (not a mix of Cytoplasmic and Extracellular residues), and check whether any of the 4 TNFRSF8 or 3 GPR65 N-glycosylation sites lie on the interface; if one does, re-fold with the core glycan attached. 2) Fold with the known TNFRSF8 ligand TNFSF8 (CD30L) included, to see whether the GPR65 interface overlaps the ligand-binding site. TNFSF8 is suggested from the UniProt function text, not from CellPhoneDB, which returned no complexes for either gene; no CORUM check was possible. 3) Confirm same-cell coexpression in activated T cells or other lymphoid/mast-cell datasets. 4) Test experimentally by co-immunoprecipitation or proximity ligation in CD30-positive lymphoid cells expressing GPR65.

BMP6 ↔ GPR31 INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE medium confidence ipTM 0.49 · pTM
Rationale
The pair is topologically possible (BMP6 is a secreted cytokine, GPR31 is a plasma-membrane GPCR) and has no trace in STRING, CellPhoneDB or PubMed, so it would be novel if real. However, the supplied fold does not support a call: ipTM is 0.49, below the 0.5 convention for a predicted interaction, interface pLDDT is only 55.8, and the minimum separation of 1.55 A with 1092 contacts and a 4819 A^2 buried surface suggests an un-relaxed, possibly clashing pose with over-extensive contacts for a GPCR ectodomain. There is also no biological precedent: UniProt describes GPR31 as a receptor for the lipid 12-(S)-HETE and a proton sensor, while BMP6 signals through serine/threonine kinase receptors (ACVR1, ACVR2B, BMPR1A) and hemojuvelin, and both proteins carry glycosylation that the fold did not model. The one positive signal is partner specificity: BMP6 passed the PAE gate against only 1 of 14 receptors tested (p_twoway=0.010074).
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt (P22004) lists BMP6 as a glycoprotein with 5 glycosylation sites and 4 disulfide bonds, and as cleaved on a pair of basic residues, meaning the mature ligand is a processed, disulfide-linked dimer. UniProt (O00270) lists GPR31 as a glycoprotein with 1 glycosylation site. The PubMed PTM search returned 146 hits for BMP6 and 2 for GPR31, none of the listed titles specifically about glycosylation of an interface. Whether any glycosylation site lies on the modeled interface could not be checked without a fold, so this is an unresolved caveat and not a demonstrated artifact. The 4819 A^2 buried surface raises the possibility that the BMP6 prodomain, which is absent from the mature ligand, contributes to the modeled contact.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for BMP6 and GPR31. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction, and GPR31 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. The pair is unreported. BMP6's documented receptors per UniProt are ACVR1, ACVR2B, BMPR1A/ALK3 and the co-receptor hemojuvelin; GPR31's documented ligands are 12-(S)-HETE and protons.
Other subunits
BMP6 (second protomer; mature ligand is a homodimer);ACVR1;ACVR2B;BMPR1A
Follow-up (not yet executed): Folding was not available in this run. Recommended: (1) re-fold mature BMP6 only (prodomain removed), as a disulfide-linked homodimer, against GPR31, and check whether ipTM exceeds 0.5 and whether the 1.55 A minimum separation / clash resolves; (2) read the interface topology to confirm contacts are restricted to GPR31 extracellular/transmembrane residues and not cytoplasmic ones; (3) check whether the BMP6 glycosylation sites or the single GPR31 glycosylation site sit on the interface, and if so re-fold with the glycan attached; (4) compare against a BMP6:ACVR1:ACVR2B positive-control fold. Bench work is not justified unless the mature-dimer fold clears ipTM 0.5 without a clash.

GHRL ↔ MRGPRX1 LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM medium confidence ipTM 0.63 · pTM
Rationale
GHRL (UniProt Q9UBU3) is a secreted hormone precursor whose mature 28-residue ghrelin peptide carries three annotated Lipidation features; UniProt states explicitly that "octanoylation is essential for the activation of the growth hormone secretagogue receptor type 1 (GHSR)" (PubMed:11306336, 35027551), i.e. the bioactive form of this ligand is an O-octanoylated peptide that AF3 cannot represent from bare sequence — on a peptide this short, the acyl chain is very likely part of, or immediately adjacent to, any modeled receptor interface. MRGPRX1 (Q96LB2) is a bona fide plasma-membrane GPCR activated promiscuously by a wide range of cationic peptides (BAM22/BAM8-22 enkephalin fragments, tick defensins, conotoxin CNF-Tx2) and even chloroquine, so a moderate-confidence fold (ipTM 0.63, interface pLDDT 70.5) of another small basic peptide into its extracellular/TM pocket is a weakly discriminating result, consistent with this partner being confident against 3 of 11 receptors tested. No prior link exists in STRING, CellPhoneDB or PubMed, so the pairing itself is novel, but the unmodeled acylation makes the modeled interface an unreliable representation of the in vivo ligand.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt GHRL: keyword "Lipoprotein", ptm_topology_feature_counts Lipidation=3, Modified residue=1 (amidation), with the FUNCTION text stating octanoylation is essential for receptor activation — this is exactly the class of modification AF3 does not model, and on a 28-residue peptide it is very likely at or near the modeled interface. UniProt MRGPRX1: keyword "Glycoprotein", Glycosylation=1 and Disulfide bond=2; a single N-glycan plus the conserved GPCR disulfides is a lesser concern (disulfides are modeled fine), though the one glycosylation site is on the extracellular face where a peptide ligand would approach. PubMed PTM searches were uninformative for both genes (GHRL hits were about tau neuropathology, chicken bursal B-cells, frailty biomarkers and the growth hormone receptor, not ghrelin acylation; MRGPRX1 hits were melanogenesis, DRG isoform sequencing, berbamine and TRPV1 signalling).
PubMed co-occurrence
pubmed_cooccurrence returned count=0 with no hits for GHRL + MRGPRX1.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING: no association found (found=false). CellPhoneDB: no curated interaction (found=false, no matches). PubMed co-occurrence for GHRL and MRGPRX1: 0 hits. The pair is therefore genuinely unreported.
Follow-up (not yet executed): Re-run AF3 with the real modification represented: model mature ghrelin-28 with the Ser3 n-octanoyl group attached (and ideally the single MRGPRX1 N-glycan) and check whether the interface survives, moves, or collapses below chain_pair_iptm 0.5 — this fold could not be run here. Also run the specificity counter-screen: acylated ghrelin against GHSR (positive control) and against two unrelated peptide GPCRs, since MRGPRX1 is known to respond to many unrelated cationic peptides. Wet-lab test would be a calcium-mobilization/IP1 assay in MRGPRX1-transfected cells with acyl- versus des-acyl ghrelin, with BAM8-22 as the positive control.

CCL11 ↔ GPR132 CONFIRMED_NOVEL VALIDATE low confidence ipTM 0.56 · pTM
Rationale
CCL11 (eotaxin, UniProt P51671) is a secreted chemokine and GPR132 (UniProt Q9UNW8) is a 7-transmembrane cell-membrane GPCR, so a secreted-ligand/membrane-receptor pairing is compartmentally plausible, and STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed co-occurrence all return nothing for this pair (0 PubMed hits), so it is novel. Support is modest: ipTM is 0.56 (just above the 0.5 threshold), interface PAE is 3.01 A, interface pLDDT is 71.1, and CCL11 was confident against only 1 of the 6 receptors it was tested with (p_twoway = 0.0499), which argues for some specificity. Confidence is low because CCL11's documented receptor is CCR3, GPR132's documented ligands are oxidized fatty acids such as 9-HODE and protons rather than any chemokine, and each protein carries one annotated glycosylation site whose position relative to the modeled interface could not be checked.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt annotates both proteins as Glycoprotein: CCL11 has 1 Glycosylation feature and 2 disulfide bonds, and GPR132 has 1 Glycosylation feature and 1 disulfide bond. The PubMed PTM search returned 74 hits for CCL11 and 1 hit for GPR132, but the titles shown are about allergic inflammation and lactate-GPR132-Src signalling, not about glycosylation of either protein, so they add nothing specific. The position of the GPR132 glycosylation site was not returned; if it lies in the extracellular N-terminus, where chemokines typically engage their receptors, it could sit at the modeled interface. This is an unverified caveat and not sufficient on its own to call the interface a PTM artifact.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for CCL11 and GPR132 returned a count of 0 with no hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING found no association between CCL11 and GPR132. CellPhoneDB found no curated interaction for the pair, and GPR132 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table at all, so that miss is uninformative for GPR132. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. There is no prior report of this pair in any of the three sources.
Follow-up (not yet executed): No fold was available in this run. Recommended next steps: (1) re-fold CCL11 with GPR132 and inspect the interface topology, to confirm that the contacts are on GPR132's extracellular N-terminus, extracellular loops and transmembrane-bundle opening and not on cytoplasmic residues, and to check whether either annotated glycosylation site lies on the interface; if the GPR132 N-glycosylation site does, re-fold with the glycan attached. (2) Fold CCL11 with its documented receptor CCR3 as a positive control, to compare ipTM against the 0.56 seen here. (3) Test experimentally with a binding or signalling assay, for example CCL11-induced Gq/inositol phosphate or beta-arrestin recruitment in GPR132-expressing cells, with 9-HODE as a positive control, bearing in mind GPR132's reported pH sensitivity.

GRIA4 ↔ GPR83 CONFIRMED_NOVEL VALIDATE low confidence ipTM 0.43 · pTM
Rationale
No database or literature source reports this pair (STRING not found, CellPhoneDB not found, PubMed co-occurrence 0), and both are plasma-membrane receptors enriched in brain and expressed in the same neuron classes, so a lateral in-membrane contact is physically possible. The structural support is weak, however: ipTM is 0.43, below the 0.5 convention for a predicted interaction, and interface pLDDT is only 63.2; the candidate rests on the interface PAE of 3.01 A and on GPR83 being confident against only 1 of the 10 receptors tested (p_twoway 0.0159). Two caveats could not be resolved without a fold: both proteins are N-glycosylated on their extracellular regions (6 sites on GRIA4, 3 on GPR83), and GRIA4 was modelled as a monomer although AMPA receptors are obligate tetramers with auxiliary subunits, so the very large 2796 A^2 interface may use surfaces that are buried in the native assembly.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt lists GRIA4 as a Glycoprotein and Lipoprotein (Palmitate) with 6 glycosylation sites, 2 lipidation sites, 1 modified residue and 2 disulfide bonds. GPR83 is a Glycoprotein with 3 glycosylation sites and 1 disulfide bond. The PubMed PTM searches returned 1 hit for GRIA4 (PMID 30692144, on Frrs1l and AMPA receptor function) and 2 for GPR83 (PMIDs 34504158 and 35559741); none of these is a site-specific glycosylation study. The glycans sit on the extracellular regions of both proteins, so if the 2796 A^2 interface involves the GRIA4 extracellular domains or the GPR83 N-terminus, it could be sterically blocked in vivo. Whether any glycosylation site lies on the modelled interface could not be checked because no fold was available in this run.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for GRIA4 and GPR83 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for GRIA4 and GPR83. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction, and GPR83 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. No source reports this pair, which supports genuine novelty.
Other subunits
GRIA2;CACNG8
Follow-up (not yet executed): 1) Inspect the existing model, or re-fold, to map the interface residues onto UniProt topology and onto the 6 GRIA4 and 3 GPR83 N-glycosylation sites; if a site is on the interface, re-fold with the core glycan attached. 2) Re-fold GPR83 against a GRIA4 homotetramer, or a GRIA2/GRIA4 heterotetramer with a TARP such as CACNG8, to test whether the contact surface is still accessible in the native assembly; GRIA2 is suggested from general AMPA receptor biology and CACNG8 from the UniProt function text, since CellPhoneDB lists no complex for either protein. 3) Because ipTM is 0.43, treat the candidate as unconfirmed until tested at the bench, for example by co-immunoprecipitation or proximity ligation in neurons or a co-transfected HEK293 system, and by testing whether GPR83 co-expression changes AMPA receptor currents.

DSC2 ↔ GPR75 IMPLAUSIBLE low confidence ipTM 0.48 · pTM
Rationale
The AF3 ipTM of 0.48 is below the 0.5 convention for a predicted interaction, and the interface pLDDT is only 62.3, even though the interaction PAE of 3.01 A passed the shortlist gate and only 1 of 9 receptors tested against this partner was confident. HPA shows no overlap in enriched expression: DSC2 is a desmosomal cadherin of stratified epithelia (esophagus 160.9 nTPM; suprabasal keratinocytes, esophageal and ocular epithelial cells), whereas GPR75 is a low-abundance brain/retina GPCR (9.8 and 7.1 nTPM) not detected in any single-cell type. STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed all return nothing for the pair, so it is not a known interaction, but nothing positive supports it either; both extracellular regions are N-glycosylated, which AF3 did not model.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt annotates DSC2 (Q02487) as a Glycoprotein with 5 glycosylation sites, and PMID 30942563 reports that incorporation of desmocollin-2 into the plasma membrane requires N-glycosylation at multiple sites. UniProt annotates GPR75 (O95800) as a Glycoprotein with 3 glycosylation sites; the PubMed hits for GPR75 concern 20-HETE signalling rather than its modifications. Any extracellular interface between the two would lie on glycosylated domains that AF3 did not model. Whether a glycosylation site falls on the modeled interface could not be checked, because no fold was available in this run.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for DSC2 and GPR75 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING found no association for the pair. CellPhoneDB found no curated interaction. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. The pair is therefore unreported, but there is also no independent supporting evidence.
Other subunits
DSG2;DSP;CCL5
Follow-up (not yet executed): Low priority. If pursued, first inspect the existing model's interface topology. The supplied buried surface area of 6222 A^2 with 1136 contacts is very large for a single-pass cadherin against a GPCR, which is my reading that the DSC2 chain may be wrapped around the receptor; check whether the interface touches both extracellular and cytoplasmic residues (physically impossible across a bilayer) and whether any of the DSC2 or GPR75 N-glycosylation sites sit on it. A re-fold with glycans attached would be the next step; fold_complex and resolve_ptm_artifact were not available in this run. CellPhoneDB lists no complex for either protein; the suggested other subunits come from the UniProt function text (DSC2's desmosomal partners DSG2 and DSP, and GPR75's reported ligand CCL5), and a CORUM/STRING complex check is still needed. Also confirm co-expression in a relevant tissue (for example retina or ocular epithelium) before any bench work.

CD244 ↔ GPR34 LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM low confidence ipTM 0.57 · pTM
Rationale
No database or literature records this pair (STRING not found, CellPhoneDB not found, PubMed co-occurrence 0), so it is novel, but the evidence does not support calling it a confirmed interaction. Both are glycosylated plasma-membrane proteins per UniProt (CD244 Q9BZW8: 8 annotated glycosylation sites; GPR34 Q9UPC5: 5 annotated glycosylation sites on a 7-TM receptor with a small extracellular surface), and AF3 modeled neither the glycans nor the bilayer. The supplied fold is only moderately confident (ipTM 0.57, interface pLDDT 66.5), and the two genes are enriched in different cell types (CD244 in NK cells and basophils; GPR34 in macrophages, microglia, Kupffer and Hofbauer cells). I could not check whether any glycosylation site actually lies on the modeled interface, so the artifact call is a judgment of plausibility, not a per-residue finding.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt lists CD244 with the keywords Glycoprotein and Phosphoprotein, 8 glycosylation sites, 4 modified residues and 1 disulfide bond. UniProt lists GPR34 with the keyword Glycoprotein, 5 glycosylation sites and 1 disulfide bond. Glycosylation sites sit on extracellular regions, so any ectodomain-to-ectodomain interface would have to accommodate up to 13 glycans that AF3 did not model; this is especially restrictive for GPR34, whose extracellular surface is small. The PubMed PTM searches returned 28 hits for CD244 and 8 for GPR34, but the titles shown concern signaling, lymphoma mutations and ligands, not site-specific glycosylation, so they add little. CD244 signaling is also phosphorylation-gated on its cytoplasmic tail (association with SH2D1A/SAP and FYN), which is unmodeled if the interface is cytoplasmic. Whether any glycosylation site lies on the modeled interface was not determined, because no per-residue interface data were available in this run.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for CD244 and GPR34 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for CD244 and GPR34. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction, and GPR34 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. The pair is unreported. UniProt gives CD48 as the ligand of CD244 and lysophosphatidylserine (a lipid) as the ligand of GPR34; neither annotation suggests a protein partner of the other's class.
Other subunits
CD48;SH2D1A
Follow-up (not yet executed): Inspect the existing AF3 model to see which regions form the interface. The supplied buried surface area of 4179 Å2 with 866 contacts is very large for a pair at ipTM 0.57 and may reflect the single CD244 transmembrane helix packing against the GPR34 7-TM bundle, which is a common result when no bilayer is modeled; this is an untested suspicion. I would have run a fold with structural analysis to obtain the interface topology (Extracellular, Transmembrane or Cytoplasmic) and to check whether the annotated N-glycosylation sites of CD244 (8) and GPR34 (5) lie on the interface, followed by a re-fold with glycans attached if they do; fold tools were not available in this run. If the interface is ectodomain-mediated and glycan-free, test it by co-immunoprecipitation or a cell-surface binding assay using CD244-Fc on GPR34-transfected cells, with CD48 as a positive control for CD244. The supplied specificity data (1 of 7 partner receptors confident, p_twoway 0.045) is marginal and should not be taken as support on its own.

IL21R ↔ GPR141 LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM low confidence ipTM 0.7 · pTM
Rationale
No prior record of this pair exists (STRING not found, CellPhoneDB not found, 0 PubMed co-mentions), and both are plasma-membrane receptors, so the compartment is compatible and the supplied fold is selective (1 of 11 partner receptors confident, p_twoway 0.027). However, both are UniProt glycoproteins (IL21R Q9HBE5 has 6 annotated glycosylation sites on its single extracellular domain; GPR141 Q7Z602 has 2), AF3 modelled neither glycan, and a 2101 A^2 interface on IL21R's extracellular domain would be hard to place clear of 6 glycans. I could not run a fold in this run, so whether any glycosylation site actually lies on the modelled interface is not established; this, together with only tissue-level (not cell-type-level) expression overlap, is why confidence is low.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt: IL21R (Q9HBE5) carries the Glycoprotein keyword with 6 annotated glycosylation sites and 3 disulfide bonds; its function is "receptor for interleukin-21". GPR141 (Q7Z602) carries the Glycoprotein keyword with 2 annotated glycosylation sites; its function is "Orphan receptor". Neither function text describes a glycan-mediated binding mechanism. The PubMed PTM search returned 40 hits for IL21R and 1 for GPR141, but the titles shown are not about glycosylation of these proteins and add nothing specific. The concern is therefore positional: 6 glycans on IL21R's single extracellular domain are unmodelled and could sterically block an extracellular interface of 2101 A^2. Whether any annotated site lies on the modelled interface was not determined, because no fold with structural analysis could be run here.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for IL21R and GPR141 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for IL21R–GPR141. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction, and GPR141 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table at all. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. The pair is novel with respect to all three sources.
Other subunits
IL2RG
Follow-up (not yet executed): 1) Map the supplied model's interface residues against the UniProt glycosylation sites and topology of Q9HBE5 and Q7Z602. If it touches both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues, treat it as a bilayer artifact. 2) If a glycosylation site is on the interface, re-fold with the core N-glycan attached at that site. 3) Re-fold IL21R + GPR141 together with IL2RG, the obligate IL-21 receptor co-chain per CellPhoneDB (complex "IL21_receptor"), to check that the GPR141 contact does not occupy the IL2RG or IL-21 binding surface. 4) Check single-cell data for co-expression of IL21R and GPR141 in the same bone-marrow or myeloid population before any co-immunoprecipitation or proximity-ligation experiment.

OLR1 ↔ GPR82 CONFIRMED_NOVEL VALIDATE low confidence ipTM 0.47 · pTM
Rationale
No database or literature source records this pair (STRING not found, CellPhoneDB not found, PubMed co-occurrence 0), both are plasma-membrane receptors, and both are enriched in the myeloid/macrophage lineage, so the interaction is novel and biologically possible. Structural support is weak, however: the supplied ipTM of 0.47 is below the 0.5 convention for a predicted interaction, and the candidate was shortlisted on interface PAE alone (3.07 A, interface pLDDT 69.4, confident against 1 of 3 receptors tested, p_twoway 0.01883). Both proteins carry extracellular N-glycosylation that AF3 did not model, and which side of the membrane the interface sits on could not be checked because no fold was available in this run.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt annotates OLR1 as Glycoprotein, Lipoprotein, Palmitate and Lectin, with 2 glycosylation sites, 2 lipidation sites and 4 disulfide bonds. Its documented ligands (oxidized LDL, advanced glycation end products, HSP70, apoptotic cells, bacteria) bind the extracellular C-type lectin-like domain. GPR82 is annotated as Glycoprotein with 2 glycosylation sites and its function is given only as "Orphan receptor". The PubMed PTM search returned 12 hits for OLR1, none of which, among the five titles shown, concerns OLR1 glycosylation at a specific site, and 0 hits for GPR82. Whether any glycosylation or palmitoylation site lies on the modeled interface is unknown, so this is an unresolved caveat and not evidence of an artifact.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for OLR1 and GPR82 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for OLR1 and GPR82. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction, and GPR82 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. With no hit in any of the three sources, the pair is novel.
Follow-up (not yet executed): A fold could not be run here. Recommended next step is to re-fold OLR1 + GPR82 and read the interface topology: confirm the contacts are extracellular–extracellular or transmembrane–transmembrane, and not a mix of cytoplasmic and extracellular residues, which would be physically impossible. The very large interface (buried surface area 3404 A^2, 557 contacts) may reflect transmembrane-helix packing, which AF3 produces without a lipid bilayer. The same fold should check whether the OLR1 or GPR82 N-glycosylation sites fall on the interface, and if so be repeated with the core glycan attached. OLR1 is a disulfide-linked homodimer, so a fold with two OLR1 chains plus GPR82 is also worth running. Because ipTM is 0.47 (below 0.5), do not commit substantial bench time before a re-fold; if it holds up, test by co-immunoprecipitation or proximity ligation in monocyte-derived macrophages or Kupffer cells.

The supplied AF3 metrics are moderate (ipTM 0.61, interface PAE 3.08 A, interface pLDDT 61.2) and partner-specific (1 of 8 receptors tested was confident, p_twoway 0.047569). Confidence is low because both proteins are glycoproteins (2 and 3 annotated glycosylation sites) whose position relative to the very large modeled interface (7143 A2 buried, 1293 contacts) could not be checked, and because the interface topology (extracellular versus cytoplasmic side) could not be verified without a fold.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt annotates both proteins with the keyword "Glycoprotein": SLITRK3 has 2 annotated glycosylation sites and GPR83 has 3 annotated glycosylation sites plus 1 disulfide bond. The PubMed PTM search returned 0 hits for SLITRK3 and 2 hits for GPR83 (PMID 34504158 and PMID 35559741), neither of which is about GPR83 glycosylation by title. Neither protein's function text describes a glycan-mediated binding mechanism. Whether any of the 5 glycosylation sites lies on the modeled interface is unknown; with 7143 A2 buried surface on extracellular-domain-containing proteins, overlap is possible and is an unresolved caveat rather than a demonstrated artifact.
PubMed co-occurrence

STRING / CellPhoneDB

No hit in any of the three sources supports genuine novelty. UniProt notes that the endogenous ligand of GPR83 remains uncertain (conflicting evidence for PEN, proCCK56-63, FAM237A and FAM237B).
Follow-up (not yet executed): A fold could not be run in this triage. Inspect the 3D model directly given the unusually large interface (7143 A2, 1293 contacts) at only moderate interface pLDDT (61.2). Wet-lab: co-immunoprecipitation or a cell-surface binding assay in neurons or transfected cells.

TNFRSF13B ↔ GPR65 CONFIRMED_NOVEL VALIDATE low confidence ipTM 0.57 · pTM
Rationale
No database or literature records this pair: STRING found nothing, CellPhoneDB found nothing, and PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. Both are membrane proteins with enriched RNA in lymphoid tissue (TNFRSF13B 29.7 nTPM, GPR65 9.1 nTPM), and the supplied fold is selective for this partner (confident against 1 of 7 receptors tested, p_twoway=0.005211, interface PAE 3.09 A, ipTM 0.57, interface pLDDT 72.2). Confidence is low because ipTM 0.57 is only just above the 0.5 threshold, the enriched single-cell types differ (B-cells/plasma cells versus mast cells), both proteins are glycoproteins, and I could not check which side of the membrane the modelled interface sits on or whether a glycosylation site lies on it.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt marks both proteins as glycoproteins: TNFRSF13B (O14836) has 1 annotated glycosylation site and 6 disulfide bonds; GPR65 (Q8IYL9) has 3 annotated glycosylation sites and 2 disulfide bonds. The PubMed PTM searches returned 3 hits for TNFRSF13B and 6 for GPR65, but none of the titles concern glycosylation of either protein itself (the TNFRSF13B hits are IgG N-glycosylation genetics and a canine transcript study; the GPR65 hits are functional studies). Neither protein's function text describes a glycan-mediated binding mechanism: TNFRSF13B binds the protein ligands TNFSF13/APRIL and TNFSF13B/BAFF, and GPR65 is a proton sensor. Glycosylation is therefore an unresolved caveat, relevant only if the modelled interface involves the extracellular domains; whether any site lies on the interface was not checked.
PubMed co-occurrence

CALCB ↔ MAS1L CONFIRMED_NOVEL VALIDATE low confidence ipTM 0.48 · pTM
Rationale
CALCB (P10092) is a secreted peptide hormone and MAS1L (P35410) is a plasma-membrane orphan GPCR, so a ligand-receptor pairing is compartmentally plausible, and STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed (0 papers) all show no prior record of the pair. The supplied screen data show selectivity on the partner side (confident against 1 of 13 receptors tested at interface PAE <= 3.75 A with no clash; PAE_interaction 3.16). Structural support is borderline, however: ipTM is 0.48, below the 0.5 convention, and interface pLDDT is 61.2, so this is a novel and plausible hypothesis, not a confident structural prediction.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt annotates MAS1L as a glycoprotein with 2 glycosylation sites; their positions relative to the modeled interface are unknown. On a GPCR such sites usually lie on the extracellular N-terminus or loops, which is where a peptide ligand would dock, so glycan interference cannot be excluded. The PubMed PTM search for MAS1L returned 0 hits. CALCB has no glycosylation; UniProt lists 1 disulfide bond and 1 modified residue (C-terminal amidation), and it is cleaved from a precursor at paired basic residues. The 3 PubMed PTM hits for CALCB do not concern modification at this interface. The glycosylation caveat is unverified and was not judged strong enough for LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for CALCB and MAS1L returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING found no association for CALCB-MAS1L. CellPhoneDB found no curated interaction (MAS1L is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table). PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. The only documented CALCB receptor per UniProt is the CALCRL-RAMP1 complex. No hit in any source supports genuine novelty.
Other subunits
RAMP1
Follow-up (not yet executed): No fold could be run in this triage, so the following are recommendations. (1) Re-fold MAS1L with only the mature, processed CGRP2 peptide (signal peptide and propeptide removed). The supplied complex size of 505 tokens and buried surface of 5512 A^2 with 1195 contacts are too large for a 37-residue peptide, which suggests the full CALCB precursor was folded and that some contacts may involve regions absent from the secreted hormone. (2) In that fold, check that the interface sits on the extracellular/transmembrane side of MAS1L and not on both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues, and whether either of the 2 MAS1L glycosylation sites lies on the interface; if one does, re-fold with the glycan attached. (3) Consider a fold with RAMP1 included, since CGRP's known receptor CALCRL requires RAMP1; this is a speculative accessory-subunit test, not a known MAS1L complex (CellPhoneDB lists no complex for either gene). (4) Because ipTM is 0.48, wet-lab confirmation is needed before any claim, for example a cAMP, calcium or beta-arrestin assay of CGRP2 on MAS1L-expressing cells, with CALCRL-RAMP1 as a positive control.

CDH2 ↔ GPR75 INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE medium confidence ipTM 0.31 · pTM
Rationale
The pair is novel (no STRING record, no CellPhoneDB record, 0 PubMed co-mentions) and compartment-compatible (both at the plasma membrane per HPA), but the fold does not support a real interface: ipTM is 0.31, below the 0.5 convention for a predicted interaction, and interface pLDDT is 58.1. The low interface PAE (3.17 A) is the only positive structural signal, and it comes with a very large buried area (7452 A^2, 1414 contacts) and a 1.6 A minimum separation that looks more like entangled or over-packed chains than a defined interface. Both proteins are also glycoproteins (7 annotated glycosylation sites on CDH2, 3 on GPR75), and I could not check whether those sites fall on the modelled interface.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt marks both proteins as glycoproteins. CDH2 (P19022) has 7 annotated glycosylation sites and 2 modified residues, and is cleaved on a pair of basic residues (propeptide removal). GPR75 (O95800) has 3 annotated glycosylation sites. These sites are expected on the extracellular regions, which is where a contact between the two would most plausibly lie, so glycan occlusion is a real but unlocalized caveat. The PubMed PTM searches (45 hits for CDH2, 11 for GPR75) returned papers on EMT markers and 20-HETE signalling, with nothing informative about glycosylation at an interface. CDH2 adhesion is also calcium-dependent, and it is unclear whether the fold included calcium ions.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for CDH2 and GPR75 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for CDH2 and GPR75. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. The pair is therefore unreported. UniProt records GPR75 as activated by the chemokine CCL5/RANTES, and CDH2 as preferentially mediating homotypic adhesion by dimerizing with CDH2 on another cell; neither annotation suggests the other protein as a partner.
Other subunits
CDH2 (homodimer partner);CCL5
Follow-up (not yet executed): Inspect the existing model directly before spending bench time. Check whether the 1.6 A minimum separation and the 7452 A^2 buried area reflect interpenetrating or over-packed chains (for example the GPR75 helical bundle wrapped by cadherin ectodomain repeats), and whether the interface mixes cytoplasmic and extracellular residues. If the model survives that, re-fold with fold_complex (not available in this run) using the mature CDH2 ectodomain (propeptide removed) with calcium against GPR75, and read the per-interface pLDDT and whether any of the 7 CDH2 or 3 GPR75 glycosylation sites sit on the interface. Only a chain-pair ipTM of 0.5 or above would justify a wet-lab test such as co-immunoprecipitation in brain or retina, where GPR75 is expressed. The supplied breadth screen (1 of 3 receptors confident at interface PAE <= 3.75 A, p_twoway = 0.054) is marginal and does not establish specificity.
