ICOSLG ↔ GPR34 LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM low confidence ipTM 0.52 · pTM
Rationale
The pair is novel (no STRING record, no CellPhoneDB record, 0 PubMed co-mentions) and plausible on compartment and cell type: both are plasma-membrane proteins per UniProt, and GPR34 is enriched in macrophage-lineage cells where ICOSLG is plausibly present. However, UniProt annotates 5 N-glycosylation sites on each protein (ICOSLG O75144, GPR34 Q9UPC5). GPR34 is a lysophosphatidylserine GPCR whose only protein-accessible extracellular surface is a short N-terminus plus three loops, so a 2816 A^2 interface with 648 contacts very plausibly overlaps glycan-occupied surface that AF3 did not model. I could not check which residues are in the interface because no fold was available in this run, so the glycan overlap is inferred rather than observed. The fold is also only marginally confident (ipTM 0.52, interface pLDDT 70.8).
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt marks both proteins with the Glycoprotein keyword and 5 annotated glycosylation sites each (ICOSLG also has 2 disulfide bonds, GPR34 has 1). The ICOSLG sites are necessarily on its extracellular Ig ectodomain, and the GPR34 sites on its small extracellular N-terminus and loops, which are the only surfaces where these two proteins could meet. The PubMed PTM searches were not informative: 1 unrelated hit for ICOSLG and 8 hits for GPR34 concerning ligands and lymphoma mutations rather than glycosylation. GPR34's documented ligand is a lipid (lysophosphatidylserine) bound within the transmembrane bundle, not a protein. Whether any glycosylation site actually lies on the modeled interface was not determined.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for ICOSLG and GPR34 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING found no association for this pair. CellPhoneDB found no curated interaction (GPR34 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table, so this miss is uninformative for GPR34). PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. The pair is unreported. The supplied breadth screen shows the partner was confident against only 1 of 11 receptors tested (interface PAE <= 3.75 A, no clash; p_twoway = 0.009381), so the signal is not generic stickiness.
Follow-up (not yet executed): Run the fold that was unavailable here: fold ICOSLG with GPR34 and read the per-residue interface against the UniProt N-glycosylation sites and membrane topology of both proteins. An interface that touches both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues, or is mostly a transmembrane-helix to transmembrane-helix contact, would point to an artifact of the missing bilayer. If a glycosylation site is on the interface, re-fold with the core N-glycan attached. If the interface survives, test at the bench by co-immunoprecipitation or a cell-surface binding assay using ICOSLG-Fc on GPR34-expressing cells, with ICOS as a competition control.

MST1 ↔ GPR37 CONFIRMED_NOVEL VALIDATE low confidence ipTM 0.52 · pTM
Rationale
No prior record of this pair exists: STRING found nothing, CellPhoneDB found nothing, and PubMed co-mention count is 0. The pairing is topologically plausible as a secreted ligand and a cell-surface GPCR: HPA classes MST1 (macrophage stimulating 1) as "Secreted to blood", and UniProt O15354 annotates GPR37 as a cell-membrane receptor that already accepts protein ligands (prosaposin, osteocalcin). Confidence is low because the fold is marginal (ipTM 0.52, interface pLDDT 70.9, minimum separation 1.98 A), GPR37 carries 3 annotated glycosylation sites whose position relative to the interface could not be checked without a fold, and the UniProt/PubMed PTM tools returned the wrong protein for the symbol "MST1".
PTM / glycosylation
GPR37 (UniProt O15354) carries the Glycoprotein keyword, 3 annotated glycosylation sites and 1 disulfide bond. Whether any of the 3 glycosylation sites lies on the modeled interface is unknown. The pubmed_ptm_glycosylation hits for GPR37 (10 papers) concern prosaposin glycosylation and signalling, not glycans on GPR37's own interface. For MST1 the tools returned the wrong protein: uniprot_annotation("MST1") resolved to Q13043, the Hippo kinase STK4 (alias "MST1"), and the 406 pubmed_ptm_glycosylation hits are likewise about the Hippo kinase. Neither is informative about the folded protein. The supplied complex_tokens=1324 equals GPR37 (613 residues) plus macrophage-stimulating protein, UniProt P26927 (711 residues), so the fold used the HGNC MST1 secreted protein and not STK4 (487 residues). From background knowledge, not from the tools, macrophage-stimulating protein is a kringle-domain, disulfide-rich plasma glycoprotein that is secreted as a single-chain precursor and activated by proteolytic cleavage; AF3 modeled it as the bare, uncleaved chain.
PubMed co-occurrence
pubmed_cooccurrence returned 0 hits for MST1 and GPR37.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
string_known_interaction returned found=false. cellphonedb_known_interaction returned found=false with no matches. pubmed_cooccurrence returned 0 papers. No source reports this pair, which is fairly strong evidence of novelty. The supplied breadth data (MST1 confident against 1 of 3 receptors tested at interface PAE <= 3.75 A with no clash; p_twoway=0.050324) indicate the fold is not a generic sticky-partner effect, though the p-value is borderline.
Other subunits
MST1R
Follow-up (not yet executed): 1) Run the fold I could not run here: AF3 on MST1 (P26927) plus GPR37 (O15354) with structural analysis, to confirm the interface is on GPR37's extracellular N-terminal domain or loops (not cytoplasmic) and to check whether any of GPR37's 3 glycosylation sites or the glycosylation sites of macrophage-stimulating protein fall on the interface. If one does, re-fold with the glycan attached. 2) Inspect the 1.98 A minimum separation for near-clashes. 3) Re-pull PTM annotation for MST1 by accession P26927, since the symbol lookup returned STK4. 4) Compare against the established receptor of macrophage-stimulating protein, MST1R (RON), which is listed in other_subunits as a positive-control comparison and not as a missing subunit (cellphonedb_complex_members found no complex for either protein): fold MST1 with MST1R and check whether the GPR37 interface reuses the RON-binding beta-chain surface. 5) Wet-lab: test binding of activated, two-chain macrophage-stimulating protein to GPR37-expressing cells, with ERK phosphorylation or receptor internalisation as readout and prosaposin or TX14A peptide as positive control.

EPHA5 ↔ GPR158 CONFIRMED_NOVEL VALIDATE low confidence ipTM 0.38 · pTM
Rationale
EPHA5 and GPR158 are both brain-enriched cell-surface membrane receptors with synaptic roles, and STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed (0 papers) all show no prior record of this pair, so it is biologically plausible and novel. The structural support is weak, however: the supplied ipTM of 0.38 is below the 0.5 convention for a predicted interaction and the interface pLDDT is only 59.4, even though the interface PAE of 3.28 A passed the shortlist gate and only 1 of 5 partner receptors tested was confident (p_twoway = 0.041). Both proteins are glycoproteins (6 and 5 annotated glycosylation sites), and I could not check whether those sites lie on the modeled interface because no fold tool was available, so this is a novel, plausible but unverified candidate rather than a structurally confirmed one.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt lists both as Glycoprotein: EPHA5 has 6 annotated glycosylation sites and GPR158 has 5, presumably on the extracellular domains. The PubMed PTM searches (7 hits for EPHA5, 3 for GPR158) returned no titles specifically about glycosylation of either protein. Neither protein's function text describes a glycan-mediated binding mechanism: EPHA5 binds GPI-anchored ephrin-A ligands, and GPR158 binds glycine, GPC4 and possibly osteocalcin. Whether any glycosylation site sits on the modeled interface could not be checked without a fold, so glycan occlusion remains an open caveat rather than a demonstrated artifact.
PubMed co-occurrence
pubmed_cooccurrence returned 0 hits for EPHA5 and GPR158.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING: no association found. CellPhoneDB: no curated interaction (GPR158 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table). PubMed co-occurrence: 0 papers. No prior record of this pair in any of the three sources, which supports genuine novelty.
Other subunits
EFNA5;RGS7;GNB5;GPC4
Follow-up (not yet executed): 1) Re-fold EPHA5 + GPR158 (fold_complex was not available in this run) and read the interface topology and PTM-at-interface output. The supplied buried surface area (7846 A^2) and 1636 contacts are very large for an ipTM of 0.38, which raises the possibility of non-specific side-by-side packing of transmembrane helices; an interface touching both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues would indicate an artifact. 2) If an N-glycosylation site lands on the interface, re-fold with the glycan attached. 3) Consider folding ectodomain-only constructs, and testing GPR158 as a homodimer (its known cryo-EM state with RGS7-GNB5), to see whether the EPHA5 contact is compatible with the known assembly. 4) Wet-lab: co-immunoprecipitation or proximity ligation in cortical or hippocampal neurons, or a cell-surface binding assay with the EPHA5 ectodomain as an Fc fusion against GPR158-expressing cells. The other_subunits listed are known partners from the UniProt function text (EFNA5 for EPHA5; RGS7, GNB5 and GPC4 for GPR158), not CellPhoneDB complex members; cellphonedb_complex_members found none for either gene.

FGFR3 ↔ GPR21 INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE low confidence ipTM 0.67 · pTM
Rationale
The pair is novel: STRING found no association, CellPhoneDB found no curated interaction, and PubMed returned 0 co-mention papers. However, the biological context needed to support it is missing or weakly negative. HPA has no subcellular localization, no single-cell detection and no protein-level data for GPR21, and its only tissue signal (blood vessel, 5.1 nTPM) does not overlap FGFR3's enriched tissues (skin 256.5, brain 148.8, esophagus 126.6 nTPM; astrocytes). Both are glycosylated membrane proteins (UniProt: 6 glycosylation sites on FGFR3, 2 on GPR21), and without a fold I cannot tell whether the modeled interface is topologically consistent or whether glycan sites sit on it, so the AF3 score (ipTM 0.67, interface PAE 3.28 Å, interface pLDDT 71.6) cannot be confirmed or attributed to an artifact.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt annotates FGFR3 (P22607) as a Glycoprotein and Phosphoprotein with 6 glycosylation sites, 6 modified residues and 3 disulfide bonds; the glycosylation sites are N-linked sites on the extracellular immunoglobulin-domain region. UniProt annotates GPR21 (Q99679) as a Glycoprotein with 2 glycosylation sites. The PubMed PTM search returned 244 hits for FGFR3 (the top hits concern kinase activation and inhibitors, not glycosylation specifically) and 1 hit for GPR21 (PMID 26934370, on plasmalogen-induced ERK/Akt signaling, not a glycosylation study). If the modeled interface involves the extracellular domains of both proteins, unmodeled glycans could plausibly occlude it, but no fold was available in this run to determine whether any glycosylation site lies on the interface, so this remains an unresolved caveat and not a demonstrated artifact.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for FGFR3 and GPR21 returned a count of 0 with no hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for FGFR3 and GPR21 (found: false). CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction (found: false, no matches), and GPR21 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table at all. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. The pair is unreported in all three sources.
Other subunits
FGF1;FGF2
Follow-up (not yet executed): No fold could be run in this triage. Recommended next steps: (1) Re-fold FGFR3 + GPR21 and read the interface topology labels to check that the contact (supplied as 4107 Å2 buried surface, 891 contacts) does not touch both Cytoplasmic and Extracellular residues at once, which would be physically impossible across a bilayer; the supplied minimum separation of 1.96 Å is tight and should also be checked for clashes. (2) In the same fold, check whether any of FGFR3's 6 or GPR21's 2 UniProt glycosylation sites sit on the interface, and if so re-fold with the core glycan attached. (3) Establish whether GPR21 and FGFR3 are actually co-expressed in any cell type (for example astrocytes, keratinocytes or vascular cells) using a full quantitative expression dataset, since HPA shows no overlap in enriched tissues. (4) The supplied specificity statistics are only modest (this partner was confident against 1 of 5 receptors tested; p_twoway = 0.053658), so a counter-screen of FGFR3 against other class A orphan GPCRs would help. The other_subunits entry (FGF1/FGF2) is from FGFR3's UniProt function text as known ligands, not from CellPhoneDB complex data, which returned no complexes for either protein; FGFR3 has 447 HPA-listed known interactions, so a CORUM/STRING complex check is also warranted.

CCK ↔ GPR6 LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM medium confidence ipTM 0.48 · pTM
Rationale
CCK (P06307) is a secreted peptide hormone and GPR6 (P46095) a plasma-membrane orphan GPCR, both enriched in brain (CCK 431.5 nTPM brain, GPR6 34.4 nTPM brain; both detected in brain inhibitory neurons), so a ligand-receptor encounter is anatomically plausible and no curated record of this pair exists. However the reported AF3 ipTM is 0.48, below the standard 0.5 AF-Multimer/AF3 convention for "an interaction is being predicted", and interface pLDDT is only 63.9 — so the model itself does not support the pair. On top of that, CCK's bioactivity is strictly PTM-gated: its UniProt keywords include Sulfation, Amidation and Glycoprotein (1 glycosylation site, 4 modified residues), and the active CCK-8 peptide requires Tyr-sulfation and C-terminal amidation for receptor binding — modifications AF3's bare-sequence model does not represent. GPR6 itself carries 3 glycosylation sites and 1 lipidation (palmitate) site and is reported to be activated by lipid-derived agonists (oleic acid, sphingosine 1-phosphate), a chemically different ligand class from a peptide hormone.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt CCK keywords: Amidation, Sulfation, Glycoprotein, Proteoglycan, Cleavage on pair of basic residues; ptm_topology_feature_counts Glycosylation 1, Modified residue 4. Tyrosine sulfation and C-terminal amidation of the mature CCK-8 peptide are the canonical determinants of CCK receptor binding and are not represented in AF3's bare-sequence input. UniProt GPR6: Glycoprotein (3 glycosylation sites), Lipoprotein/Palmitate (1 lipidation), Phosphoprotein. The CCK PubMed PTM search returned 3242 hits but the titles shown are generic pathway papers and unreliable; GPR6 returned only 8, none PTM-specific. The UniProt annotation is the meaningful signal here.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING: no association found for CCK-GPR6 (found=false). CellPhoneDB: no curated interaction (found=false), and GPR6 is not even in the CellPhoneDB gene table. PubMed co-occurrence: 1 hit (PMID 18588537, "Mu-opioid receptor activation induces transcriptional plasticity in the central extended amygdala"), which is a transcriptomic study co-mentioning both genes, not evidence of binding. So the pair is genuinely unreported.
Other subunits
GNAS
Follow-up (not yet executed): fold_complex was unavailable in this run. Recommended folds: (1) re-run CCK + GPR6 using only the mature sulfated/amidated CCK-8 octapeptide rather than the full preprocholecystokinin sequence, with the sulfotyrosine and C-terminal amide represented, to test whether the interface survives in the physiologically active ligand state; (2) if an interface emerges, check with structural_analysis whether GPR6's three N-glycosylation sites or the palmitoylation site land on it, and whether contacts fall inside the transmembrane bundle (the orthosteric GPCR site) rather than only on extracellular loops. Bench-side, the decisive test is a cAMP/GloSensor or BRET assay in GPR6-transfected cells challenged with sulfated CCK-8, against a GPR6-null control, since GPR6 is constitutively active via G(s) and any agonist claim needs an above-basal response.

GHRL ↔ GPR32 LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM medium confidence ipTM 0.49 · pTM
Rationale
AF3 ipTM=0.49 is below the standard 0.5 AF-Multimer/AF3 threshold for "predicting a real interaction", so the model itself does not support this pair, and the interface pLDDT of 67.2 is only moderate. More decisively, UniProt (Q9UBU3) states that octanoylated ghrelin-28 is the predominant form and that "octanoylation is essential for the activation of the growth hormone secretagogue receptor type 1 (GHSR)" (PubMed:11306336, 35027551), with 3 annotated Lipidation features — a lipid modification AF3 does not represent at all, meaning the modeled bare-peptide interface is unlikely to correspond to the real receptor-engaging form. GPR32 (O75388) is itself a receptor for a lipid mediator (resolvin D1) and carries 2 glycosylation sites, adding a second unmodeled-modification caveat on the extracellular face where a peptide would dock.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt GHRL: keyword "Lipoprotein", Lipidation x3, and the FUNCTION text explicitly makes Ser3 octanoylation essential for receptor activation — a mechanism AF3's bare sequence cannot represent. UniProt GPR32: keyword "Glycoprotein", Glycosylation x2 plus 1 disulfide bond, on a receptor whose documented high-affinity ligand is the lipid mediator resolvin D1. pubmed_ptm_glycosylation returned 5 hits for GHRL and 7 for GPR32, but those titles are mostly about ghrelin physiology and resolvin D1 signalling rather than site-specific PTM mapping, so UniProt is the load-bearing evidence.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING: no association found for GHRL–GPR32. CellPhoneDB: no curated interaction (GPR32 is not even in the CellPhoneDB gene table). PubMed co-occurrence: 0 hits. So the pair is genuinely unreported — novelty is not the problem here; mechanistic plausibility of the modeled interface is.
Follow-up (not yet executed): fold_complex was unavailable in this run; the fold I would have run is GHRL(ghrelin-28 mature peptide) + GPR32 with the Ser3 n-octanoyl group explicitly represented as a covalent ligand, to see whether the acylated peptide reaches chain_pair_iptm >= 0.5 and whether the two GPR32 N-glycosylation sites land on the modeled interface. Also worth checking that the contacts fall inside the transmembrane bundle (orthosteric site) rather than only on extracellular loops. Wet-lab: a GPR32 calcium/cAMP or beta-arrestin assay with acyl-ghrelin versus des-acyl ghrelin, with GHSR as positive control and resolvin D1 as the GPR32 control agonist.

CFC1 ↔ GPR148 INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE low confidence ipTM 0.53 · pTM
Rationale
The pair is topologically plausible and has no prior record: CFC1 is a secreted/GPI-anchored extracellular protein, GPR148 is a plasma-membrane orphan GPCR, and STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed all return nothing for the pair. It cannot be called a confirmed novel interaction because HPA reports GPR148 RNA as "Not detected" in every tissue and cell type, so there is no evidence the receptor is expressed where CFC1 is (pituitary, brain, pancreatic islets). The fold itself is marginal (ipTM 0.53, interface pLDDT 61.5), CFC1 was also confident against 3 of the 14 receptors tested, and both proteins carry an N-glycosylation site whose position relative to the interface could not be checked because no fold tool was available in this run.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt (CFC1, P0CG37) lists the keywords Glycoprotein, GPI-anchor and Lipoprotein, with 1 glycosylation site, 1 lipidation site and 3 disulfide bonds. UniProt (GPR148, Q8TDV2) lists Glycoprotein with 1 glycosylation site. The PubMed PTM search returned 4 hits for CFC1, none of which is clearly about CFC1 glycosylation, and 0 hits for GPR148. CFC1 is a small EGF-CFC domain protein, so a single N-glycan or the GPI anchor could plausibly sit near a 2486 Å² interface, but whether either site is on the modeled interface is unknown. This is an unresolved caveat, not established evidence of an artifact.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for CFC1 and GPR148 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING found no association for CFC1 and GPR148. CellPhoneDB has no curated interaction for the pair, and GPR148 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. The pair is unreported; GPR148 is annotated in UniProt as an orphan receptor.
Other subunits
NODAL;ACVR1B;ACVR2B
Follow-up (not yet executed): 1. Fold CFC1 with GPR148 and read the structural analysis: check that the interface is on GPR148's extracellular or transmembrane residues and not its cytoplasmic side, and whether the CFC1 or GPR148 N-glycosylation site or the CFC1 GPI-attachment region lies on the interface. If a glycan site is on the interface, re-fold with the glycan attached. 2. Counter-screen for specificity: CFC1 was confident against 3 of 14 receptors, so compare the GPR148 interface with the other two confident receptors to see whether CFC1 uses the same surface on all three. 3. Fold CFC1 with its known partners (NODAL, ACVR1B, ACVR2B; CellPhoneDB complex NODAL_receptor_IbIIb_CFC1) to see whether the GPR148-contacting surface overlaps the canonical NODAL/ACVR1B binding surface. 4. Before bench work, confirm GPR148 is expressed in a relevant tissue (for example by qPCR or public brain/testis/pituitary datasets), since HPA does not detect it.

CLSTN1 ↔ GPR22 CONFIRMED_NOVEL VALIDATE low confidence ipTM 0.49 · pTM
Rationale
No prior record of this pair exists: STRING found nothing, CellPhoneDB found nothing, and PubMed returned 0 co-mentions. The pair is biologically plausible, since both are plasma-membrane proteins per UniProt and both are present in brain (GPR22 RNA is enhanced in excitatory and inhibitory neurons; CLSTN1 is broadly expressed with protein detected in cerebral cortex), and the supplied screen shows the fold was confident for only 1 of 8 receptors tested with this partner (p_twoway=0.017549). Confidence is low because the ipTM of 0.49 is below the 0.5 convention for a predicted interaction, the interface pLDDT is only 60.0, HPA has no subcellular data for either protein, and both carry extracellular N-glycosylation sites whose position relative to the modeled interface could not be checked without a fold.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt marks both proteins as glycoproteins: CLSTN1 has 3 annotated glycosylation sites and GPR22 has 2. These sites would lie on the extracellular regions, which AF3 folded without glycans. The PubMed PTM searches returned 3 hits for CLSTN1 and 2 for GPR22, none of which actually concern glycosylation of these proteins. No binding mechanism in either UniProt function text is glycan- or lipid-mediated (CLSTN1 is a calcium-dependent adhesion molecule that binds neurexins and KLC1). Whether any glycosylation site sits on the modeled interface is unknown, so this is an unresolved caveat and not grounds for an artifact call.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for CLSTN1 and GPR22 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for CLSTN1 and GPR22. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction, and GPR22 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. GPR22 is an orphan receptor per UniProt, with only 2 known interactions in HPA; CLSTN1 has 25. Taken together this is fairly strong evidence that the pair is unreported.
Other subunits
NRXN1;KLC1;APBA2;APP
Follow-up (not yet executed): A fold could not be run in this triage. Recommended next steps: (1) Re-fold CLSTN1 with GPR22 and read the interface topology, to confirm the contact is extracellular-to-extracellular or cytoplasmic-to-cytoplasmic and does not touch both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues at once; the very large interface (buried surface 5160 Å2, 1015 contacts across 1414 tokens) makes this check important. (2) In the same fold, check whether any of the 3 CLSTN1 or 2 GPR22 N-glycosylation sites lie on the interface, and if so re-fold with the glycans attached. (3) Do not treat the ipTM of 0.49 as a positive signal on its own; the candidate rests on the interface PAE of 3.41 Å and the 1-of-8 receptor specificity. (4) If the fold holds up, test by co-immunoprecipitation or proximity labelling in neurons or cardiomyocytes. The listed other subunits are known CLSTN1 partners from its UniProt function text, not CellPhoneDB complex members (neither gene has a curated CellPhoneDB complex); a general complex-database check such as CORUM was not available.

CFC1 ↔ GPR33 INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE medium confidence ipTM 0.58 · pTM
Rationale
The pair is novel in every database checked (STRING not found, CellPhoneDB not found, 0 PubMed co-mentions) and is topologically possible as a secreted/GPI-anchored ligand (CFC1) meeting a plasma-membrane GPCR (GPR33). However, HPA reports GPR33 RNA as "Not detected" in every tissue and every single-cell type, and HPA has no subcellular location data for either protein, so there is no evidence the receptor is ever expressed where CFC1 could reach it. The fold itself is only marginal (ipTM 0.58, interface pLDDT 64.1), CFC1 was confident against 3 of the 14 receptors it was tested against, and both extracellular surfaces carry unmodelled glycans (GPR33 has 4 annotated glycosylation sites; CFC1 has 1 plus a GPI anchor).
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt annotates GPR33 (Q49SQ1) as a Glycoprotein with 4 glycosylation sites, and CFC1 (P0CG37) as a Glycoprotein and Lipoprotein with 1 glycosylation site, 1 lipidation site (GPI anchor) and 3 disulfide bonds. For a secreted ligand binding a GPCR, the modeled interface would be on the GPCR's extracellular face, where the receptor's glycans sit, so the glycosylation is plausibly relevant to the interface, but whether any site lies on the modeled contact residues could not be checked without a fold. The PubMed PTM search returned 0 hits for GPR33 and 4 hits for CFC1, none of which is about CFC1 protein glycosylation at a binding interface (one neuroblastoma stemness paper; the others are unrelated antibody and sea cucumber cerebroside papers).
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for CFC1 and GPR33 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for CFC1 and GPR33 (found: false). CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction for the pair, and GPR33 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table at all. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. The pair is not previously reported in any of the three sources.
Other subunits
NODAL;ACVR1B;ACVR2A;ACVR2B;ACVR1C
Follow-up (not yet executed): 1) Before any bench work, confirm that the GPR33 allele in the intended experimental system encodes an intact, expressed receptor: HPA shows no detectable RNA, and the gene is, as far as I recall, a pseudogene in most humans. 2) If an intact receptor exists, run an AF3 fold of CFC1 + GPR33 and read the interface topology (contacts should be on GPR33's extracellular or transmembrane residues only) and whether any of GPR33's 4 glycosylation sites or CFC1's single site lies on the interface; if one does, re-fold with the glycan attached. 3) The supplied breadth figure (CFC1 confident against 3 of 14 receptors at interface PAE <= 3.75 A) suggests limited specificity, so compare this interface with the other two confident receptors. 4) CFC1's established role is as a NODAL co-receptor in CellPhoneDB complexes with ACVR1B/ACVR2A, ACVR1B/ACVR2B and ACVR1C/ACVR2B; these are its known partners and alternative type I/II receptor combinations, not subunits to add to this fold all at once.

FLT1 ↔ GPR4 CONFIRMED_NOVEL VALIDATE low confidence ipTM 0.6 · pTM
Rationale
No database records this pair (STRING found nothing, CellPhoneDB found nothing, and the single PubMed co-mention is a VEGFA gene-network analysis, not a binding study), so the interaction is novel. It is physically plausible because both are plasma-membrane receptors with vascular endothelial biology: FLT1 is enhanced in vascular endothelial cells (1253.8 nCPM) and UniProt describes GPR4 acting in vascular endothelial cells. Confidence is low because HPA has no expression or localisation data for GPR4, the supplied AF3 metrics are only moderate (ipTM 0.6, interface pLDDT 63.7), and both proteins are glycoproteins (13 annotated glycosylation sites on FLT1, 2 on GPR4) whose position relative to the modelled interface could not be checked without a fold.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt marks both proteins with the Glycoprotein keyword: FLT1 has 13 annotated glycosylation sites, 6 disulfide bonds and 8 modified residues; GPR4 has 2 annotated glycosylation sites and 2 disulfide bonds. The PubMed PTM searches (208 hits for FLT1, 14 for GPR4) returned no titles specifically about glycosylation gating an interaction. If the modelled interface involves the FLT1 extracellular domain or the GPR4 extracellular face, glycans that AF3 did not model could occlude it. Whether any site sits on the interface was not determined, because no fold with per-residue analysis was available in this run.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed returned 1 co-mention (PMID 34870494), a systems analysis of VEGFA regulation networks in renal cell carcinoma. It appears unrelated to a direct FLT1-GPR4 interaction.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for FLT1 and GPR4. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction, and GPR4 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table. PubMed co-occurrence returned 1 hit (PMID 34870494, "System analysis of VEGFA in renal cell carcinoma"), which is a bioinformatic gene-network co-mention and not evidence of direct binding.
Other subunits
KDR
Follow-up (not yet executed): Re-fold FLT1 + GPR4 with per-residue interface analysis to establish (a) whether the interface is extracellular, transmembrane or cytoplasmic, and whether it mixes cytoplasmic and extracellular residues, which would be physically impossible; and (b) whether any of the 13 FLT1 or 2 GPR4 glycosylation sites lie on the interface. If one does, re-fold with the glycan attached. The supplied minimum separation of 2.14 Å and interface pLDDT of 63.7 should also be inspected for local clashes. CellPhoneDB lists KDR as a FLT1 complex partner (FLT1 heterodimerises with KDR), so a FLT1 + KDR + GPR4 fold is a possible secondary test, though nothing here indicates KDR is required. Wet-lab confirmation would be co-immunoprecipitation or proximity ligation in primary endothelial cells, where GPR4 expression should first be verified given the absent HPA data.

SST ↔ MRGPRX2 INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE medium confidence ipTM 0.26 · pTM
Rationale
The AF3 model itself does not support this pair: ipTM = 0.26, far below the standard 0.5 AF-Multimer/AF3 convention for "a real interaction is being predicted", with only moderate interface pLDDT (64.1) and a minimum separation of 1.94 A that suggests an un-relaxed, partly clashing interface despite the large buried area (2329 A^2, 432 contacts). Biologically the pairing is not absurd — SST (P61278) is a secreted hormone/plasma protein and MRGPRX2 (Q96LB1) is a mast-cell-specific plasma-membrane GPCR whose UniProt FUNCTION explicitly lists cortistatin-14 (a close somatostatin paralog), substance P, PAMP-12, LL-37 and dynorphins as basic-secretagogue peptide agonists, so a somatostatin-MRGPRX2 agonism hypothesis is chemically coherent — but nothing in STRING, CellPhoneDB or PubMed records this specific pair, and the fold cannot be used as positive evidence at this confidence. Verdict is therefore "not settled by this evidence" rather than novel or artifactual.
PTM / glycosylation
Neither protein raises a glycan-mediated-mechanism concern. SST's UniProt ptm_topology_feature_counts show only 1 Modified residue (C-terminal amidation) and 1 Disulfide bond — no Glycosylation or Lipidation; MRGPRX2 shows only 2 Disulfide bonds and no annotated Glycosylation. The PubMed PTM searches returned 126 (SST) and 41 (MRGPRX2) hits, but the visible titles are neuroscience/mast-cell pharmacology papers unrelated to modification of a binding interface. Note AF3 would not model SST's C-terminal amidation, which is required for somatostatin bioactivity, but that is a small modification unlikely to abolish a whole interface.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING: no association found for SST-MRGPRX2 (found=false, no channels). CellPhoneDB: no curated interaction (found=false), and MRGPRX2 is not even in the CellPhoneDB gene table, so a miss there is uninformative. PubMed co-occurrence: 0 papers mentioning both genes. No evidence this specific pair is already known.
Other subunits
GNAQ;GNAI1
Follow-up (not yet executed): Could not run fold_complex in this environment. Recommended fold: SST-14 (the mature, processed peptide, not the prepro sequence) against MRGPRX2, since the 446-token complex here likely included the propeptide, which would distort the pose; check whether contacts fall inside the transmembrane bundle/ECL2 (the orthosteric secretagogue pocket shared by substance P and cortistatin-14) rather than on cytoplasmic or purely extracellular-loop residues. Because cortistatin-14 is an established MRGPRX2 agonist and is highly homologous to somatostatin-14, the decisive experiment is a direct bench assay: beta-hexosaminidase release / calcium flux in MRGPRX2-transfected cells or LAD2 mast cells stimulated with SST-14, with cortistatin-14 as positive control and MRGPRX2-knockout cells as specificity control.

The inflammatory response can then activate or sensitize nociceptors, promoting pain (By similarity). SPX ↔ MRGPRX2 CONFIRMED_NOVEL VALIDATE low confidence ipTM 0.39 · pTM
Rationale
SPX (Q9BT56, spexin) is an HPA-annotated secreted blood hormone and MRGPRX2 (Q96LB1) is a mast-cell-specific plasma-membrane GPCR, so a secreted-peptide/receptor pairing is compartment-plausible and does not require same-cell coexpression; MRGPRX2's UniProt FUNCTION explicitly describes it as a promiscuous receptor for endogenous peptide secretagogues (substance P, cortistatin-14, PAMP-12, LL-37, beta-defensins, dynorphins, CXCL17), which makes a short secreted peptide like spexin a mechanistically reasonable candidate agonist. No prior evidence exists anywhere: STRING returned found=false, CellPhoneDB returned no match, and pubmed_cooccurrence returned 0 hits for SPX + MRGPRX2 — genuine novelty. Confidence is low because the AF3 ipTM of 0.39 is below the 0.50 AF-Multimer/AF3 convention for "a real interaction is predicted here" and the interface pLDDT is only 63.5, so the model itself does not support the pose despite the large buried area (3907 A^2) and favourable interface PAE (3.51 A).
PTM / glycosylation
Neither protein carries a glycosylation or lipidation concern: SPX ptm_topology_feature_counts shows only 1 Modified residue, and MRGPRX2 shows only 2 Disulfide bonds plus Transmembrane/Topological domain features, with no Glycosylation entries for either. The one relevant caveat is that SPX's UniProt keywords include "Amidation" and "Cleavage on pair of basic residues" — the bioactive species is the processed, C-terminally amidated 14-mer spexin, not the bare precursor sequence AF3 was given, and C-terminal amidation is frequently required for peptide-GPCR agonism. The pubmed_ptm_glycosylation hits were largely off-target (SPX query returned tau/P-tau217 and plant-nutrient papers; MRGPRX2 query returned mast-cell pharmacology papers), so UniProt is the reliable signal here.
PubMed co-occurrence
pubmed_cooccurrence returned count=0 with no hits for SPX and MRGPRX2 — the strongest available indicator of genuine novelty for this pair.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
string_known_interaction: found=false (no channel scores at all). cellphonedb_known_interaction: found=false, no matches. pubmed_cooccurrence: count=0, no hits. cellphonedb_complex_members for MRGPRX2: gene not in the CellPhoneDB gene table, so no known complex partners to add. SPX's documented receptors are the galanin receptors GALR2 and GALR3 (UniProt, PubMed:17284679, PubMed:24517231) — MRGPRX2 is not among them. Zero evidence in all three sources means this pair is novel rather than already known.
Follow-up (not yet executed): Recommended fold (not runnable in this session): re-run AF3 with only the mature processed spexin 14-mer (NWTPQAMLYLKGAQ-NH2 region) rather than the full prepropeptide, since the signal peptide and propeptide flanks likely degrade the interface, and check whether ipTM rises above 0.5 and whether contacts fall inside the MRGPRX2 transmembrane bundle (the orthosteric site used by substance P and PAMP-12) rather than only on extracellular loops. Also fold the same mature peptide against GALR2 as a positive control and against two unrelated GPCRs as a specificity counter-screen. Wet-lab: beta-arrestin recruitment or calcium-flux assay in MRGPRX2-transfected cells with synthetic amidated spexin, plus a mast cell beta-hexosaminidase release assay.

CD226 ↔ GPR141 LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM low confidence ipTM 0.64 · pTM
Rationale
The pair is novel (no STRING, CellPhoneDB or PubMed record) and compartment-compatible, since both are plasma-membrane proteins of haematopoietic cells. However, UniProt annotates 8 N-glycosylation sites on CD226 (Q15762) and 2 on GPR141 (Q7Z602), and AF3 modelled neither set of glycans. With a supplied buried surface of 3242 Å2 and 655 contacts, the interface is large enough that overlap with a glycan site is plausible, but I could not verify this: no fold was available in this run, so whether any glycosylation site actually sits on the interface is unknown. The verdict is a caveat on an otherwise plausible novel candidate, not a demonstrated artifact.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt lists CD226 (Q15762) with the keywords Glycoprotein and Phosphoprotein, 8 Glycosylation features, 3 disulfide bonds and 2 modified residues; the glycosylation sites of a single-pass receptor of this type lie in its extracellular immunoglobulin domains, which are also where it binds its known ligands. UniProt lists GPR141 (Q7Z602) as a Glycoprotein with 2 Glycosylation features. The PubMed PTM search returned 36 hits for CD226, but the titles shown are mostly not about CD226 glycosylation itself, and 1 hit for GPR141 that is unrelated to glycosylation; the UniProt annotation is the reliable signal here. Whether any of these sites lies on the modelled interface was not determined. CD226 signalling is also phosphorylation-dependent (Src-family kinase phosphorylation enabling GRB2 binding), but that concerns its cytoplasmic tail and is not obviously relevant to this interface.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for CD226 and GPR141 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for CD226 and GPR141. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction, and GPR141 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table at all. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. Nothing in these three sources indicates the pair is already known. GPR141 is an orphan receptor per UniProt, and the documented CD226 ligands are PVR/CD155 and NECTIN2/CD112.
Other subunits
PVR;NECTIN2
Follow-up (not yet executed): Not run here because fold_complex and resolve_ptm_artifact were unavailable: (1) re-fold CD226 with GPR141 and read the structural analysis to see whether any of the 8 CD226 or 2 GPR141 N-glycosylation sites lies on the interface, and whether the interface mixes cytoplasmic and extracellular residues or is mainly transmembrane-helix packing, which AF3 can produce without a bilayer; (2) if a glycan site is on the interface, re-fold with the core glycan attached. Also check whether the contact overlaps the CD226 surface that binds PVR/CD155 and NECTIN2/CD112 (listed in other_subunits as known ligands that may compete, not as missing complex members). The supplied specificity screen is only marginal (1 of 13 partner receptors confident, p_twoway = 0.052919), and the supplied interface pLDDT of 71.0 and ipTM of 0.64 are moderate. If the fold checks pass, test binding in cells that carry native glycosylation, for example co-immunoprecipitation or a cell-surface binding assay with GPR141-expressing myeloid cells and CD226-Fc.

LRP8 ↔ GPR3 INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE low confidence ipTM 0.48 · pTM
Rationale
The LRP8-GPR3 pair is novel in every database checked (STRING not found, CellPhoneDB not found, 0 PubMed co-mentions) and both are cell-membrane receptors per UniProt, so the pairing is not topologically impossible. However, the evidence does not positively support it: the AF3 ipTM is 0.48, below the 0.5 convention for a predicted interaction, HPA has no subcellular immunofluorescence data for either protein, and HPA shows no shared enriched tissue or cell type. Both proteins are glycoproteins (LRP8 6 annotated glycosylation sites, GPR3 1 site plus 1 palmitoylation site), and whether any of these sit on the modeled interface could not be checked because no fold was available in this run.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt annotates LRP8 as a Glycoprotein with 6 glycosylation sites and 27 disulfide bonds in its extracellular domain, and as a Phosphoprotein. UniProt annotates GPR3 as a Glycoprotein with 1 glycosylation site, a Lipoprotein with 1 palmitoylation (lipidation) site, and 3 modified residues. The PubMed PTM searches returned 12 hits for LRP8 and 6 for GPR3, but the titles shown are not specifically about glycosylation of an interface region. Whether any glycosylation site lies on the modeled interface is unknown without a fold, so this is an unresolved caveat and not an established artifact.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for LRP8 and GPR3 returned a count of 0 with no hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING found no association between LRP8 and GPR3. CellPhoneDB has no curated interaction for the pair, and GPR3 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table at all. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. The pair is therefore unreported, which is fairly strong evidence of novelty but not of a real interaction.
Other subunits
RELN;DAB1;APOE;GNAS
Follow-up (not yet executed): Re-fold LRP8 with GPR3 and inspect the structural analysis for three things: (1) interface topology, to confirm the contacts are not touching both Cytoplasmic and Extracellular residues at once and are not simply transmembrane helices packed together in the absence of a bilayer (the supplied buried surface area of 3870 A^2 with 707 contacts is large enough to raise that concern); (2) whether any of LRP8's 6 glycosylation sites or GPR3's single site lies on the interface, and if so re-fold with the glycan attached; (3) whether ipTM rises above 0.5 when the fold is restricted to the interacting domains instead of the full 1293-token complex. The supplied minimum separation of 1.94 A should also be checked for a clash. The other_subunits listed are known functional partners from UniProt (Reelin, DAB1 and apoE for LRP8; Gs alpha for GPR3), not curated complex members; CellPhoneDB lists no complex for either protein, and a CORUM check is still needed. If the fold holds up, test by co-immunoprecipitation or proximity ligation in neurons or a co-transfected cell line.

CD74 ↔ GPR75 LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM low confidence ipTM 0.42 · pTM
Rationale
The pair is unreported (STRING not found, CellPhoneDB not found, 0 PubMed co-mentions) and topologically possible, since CD74 is a single-pass membrane/secreted protein that reaches the cell surface and GPR75 is a plasma-membrane GPCR. However, the AF3 ipTM of 0.42 is below the 0.5 convention for a predicted interaction, and only the interface PAE (3.56 A) passed the shortlist gate. UniProt annotates CD74 (P04233) as a glycoprotein and proteoglycan with 6 glycosylation sites and GPR75 (O95800) with 3 glycosylation sites, so a modeled interface as large as this one (3837 A2 buried, 671 contacts, interface pLDDT 65.3) is plausibly occupied by glycans that the bare-sequence model omits. Whether any glycosylation site actually lies on the interface could not be checked without a fold, so this is a low-confidence call.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt annotates CD74 (P04233) with the keywords Glycoprotein, Proteoglycan and Phosphoprotein, and with 6 Glycosylation features, 3 disulfide bonds and 1 modified residue. UniProt annotates GPR75 (O95800) as a Glycoprotein with 3 Glycosylation features. The PubMed PTM search returned 135 hits for CD74 and 11 for GPR75, but the top titles concern MIF-CD74 signalling and 20-HETE/GPR75 signalling rather than glycosylation, so they add nothing specific. Glycans on CD74's extracellular/luminal domain and on GPR75's extracellular regions are plausibly relevant to an interface this large (3837 A2), but the positions of the glycosylation sites relative to the modeled contact residues were not verified.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for CD74 and GPR75 returned a count of 0, with no hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for CD74 and GPR75. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. The pair is novel as far as these sources show. UniProt lists CCL5/RANTES as the activating ligand of GPR75 and MIF as the cytokine ligand of CD74; neither annotation links the two proteins.
Other subunits
HLA-DRA;HLA-DRB1;MIF
Follow-up (not yet executed): Inspect the existing AF3 model for two things: whether any of the 6 CD74 or 3 GPR75 UniProt glycosylation sites sit on the interface, and whether the contact is mainly CD74's single transmembrane helix packed against the GPR75 helical bundle, which is a common bilayer-free artifact. Also check that the interface does not mix cytoplasmic and extracellular residues. If a fold becomes available, re-fold with the N-glycans attached at their UniProt sites, and fold CD74 as its native trimer; in vivo CD74 is normally trimeric and bound to MHC class II alpha/beta chains, which may occupy the modeled surface (the listed subunits come from the UniProt function text, not from CellPhoneDB, which returned no complexes for either protein). The supplied specificity result (1 of 11 partner receptors confident, p_twoway=0.003574) is encouraging, but because ipTM is below 0.5, a co-immunoprecipitation or BRET test in cells expressing both proteins is needed before treating this as a real interaction.

CFC1 ↔ GPR32 INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE medium confidence ipTM 0.45 · pTM
Rationale
The pair is novel (no STRING record, no CellPhoneDB record, 0 PubMed co-mentions) and topologically possible (secreted/GPI-anchored CFC1 against the extracellular face of the plasma-membrane GPCR GPR32), but nothing positively supports it. The supplied AF3 ipTM of 0.45 is below the 0.5 convention for a predicted interaction, although the interface PAE of 3.56 A passes the shortlist gate of 3.75 A; CFC1 also passed that gate against 3 of the 14 receptors tested, so the signal is not unique to GPR32. HPA has no subcellular data for either protein, there is no shared enriched tissue or cell type, GPR32's documented ligands are lipid mediators (resolvin D1) rather than proteins, and both proteins carry extracellular glycosylation that the fold did not model.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt annotates CFC1 as a glycoprotein and GPI-anchored lipoprotein (1 glycosylation site, 1 lipidation site, 3 disulfide bonds) and GPR32 as a glycoprotein (2 glycosylation sites, 1 disulfide bond). GPCR N-glycosylation sites typically sit on the extracellular N-terminus or loops, which is the only face CFC1 could reach, so unmodeled glycans could plausibly affect the modeled interface; whether any site lies on the actual contact residues is unknown without a fold-level structural analysis. The PubMed PTM searches (4 hits for CFC1, 7 for GPR32) returned no titles specifically about glycosylation of either protein at a relevant site; the GPR32 hits are resolvin D1 pharmacology papers.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for CFC1 and GPR32 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for CFC1 and GPR32. CellPhoneDB has no curated interaction for the pair, and GPR32 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table at all. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. The pair is unreported in all three sources.
Other subunits
NODAL;ACVR1B;ACVR2A;ACVR2B;ACVR1C
Follow-up (not yet executed): Folding tools were not available in this run, so no fold was performed. Recommended next steps: (1) re-fold CFC1 with GPR32 and read the interface topology, to confirm the contacts fall on GPR32 extracellular or transmembrane-boundary residues and not on cytoplasmic residues, and to check whether GPR32's 2 glycosylation sites or CFC1's 1 glycosylation site sit on the interface; (2) compare against CFC1 folded with its established partners (NODAL with ACVR1B and ACVR2B) as a positive control, and inspect the other 2 receptors that passed the PAE gate to see whether CFC1's EGF-like/CFC domain docks generically on GPCR extracellular faces; (3) before any bench work, confirm that both proteins are actually present in a shared tissue, since the HPA enriched-tissue profiles do not overlap. CellPhoneDB's CFC1 complexes are with mutually exclusive receptor paralog combinations (ACVR1B+ACVR2A, ACVR1B+ACVR2B, ACVR1C+ACVR2B), so fold only one representative set.

IL36RN ↔ GPR87 LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM low confidence ipTM 0.67 · pTM
Rationale
IL36RN (secreted IL-1-family cytokine) and GPR87 (cell-membrane GPCR) are a compartment-plausible secreted-ligand/receptor pair with no prior report: STRING found nothing, CellPhoneDB found nothing and PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. However, UniProt Q9BY21 annotates GPR87 as a Glycoprotein with 3 glycosylation sites, which must lie on the extracellular face, the only side a secreted cytokine can reach. The modeled interface is large (2618 A^2 buried, 526 contacts) and was folded on bare sequence, so glycan occlusion is plausible but not shown, because no fold tool was available to check whether the sites sit on the interface. The supplied fold evidence is moderately specific (ipTM 0.67, interface PAE 3.57 A, interface pLDDT 70.2, confident against 1 of 5 receptors tested, p_twoway 0.045), which is why this is a low-confidence artifact call and not a dismissal.
PTM / glycosylation
GPR87 (UniProt Q9BY21): keyword Glycoprotein, 3 annotated glycosylation sites and 1 disulfide bond. The glycosylation sites are necessarily extracellular, on the same face as any cytokine-binding interface, but their position relative to the modeled interface residues was not checked. IL36RN (UniProt Q9UBH0): no glycosylation annotated, 1 disulfide bond only, which is not a concern. The PubMed PTM searches (2 hits for IL36RN, 7 for GPR87) returned no titles about glycosylation of either protein and add nothing.
PubMed co-occurrence
pubmed_cooccurrence returned 0 hits for IL36RN and GPR87.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING: no association found. CellPhoneDB: no interaction found, and GPR87 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table. PubMed co-occurrence: 0 papers. The pair is novel as far as these sources show. UniProt gives IL36RN's known receptor as IL1RL2 (with possible SIGIRR recruitment) and GPR87's known ligand as lysophosphatidic acid, a lipid; a protein cytokine binding a lipid-liganded class A GPCR would be mechanistically unusual.
Other subunits
IL1RL2;IL1RAP;SIGIRR
Follow-up (not yet executed): 1. Map the 3 UniProt N-glycosylation sites of GPR87 (Q9BY21) against the AF3 interface residues. If any falls on the interface, re-fold with the core glycan attached and see whether the interface survives. 2. Inspect the interface topology in the existing model to confirm that IL36RN contacts only extracellular and transmembrane-boundary residues of GPR87, not cytoplasmic loops. 3. Fold IL36RN with its canonical receptor IL1RL2 as a positive control, to compare interface confidence and to see whether the same IL36RN surface is used. The listed other subunits (IL1RL2, IL1RAP, SIGIRR) are IL36RN's known receptor system from UniProt, not evidence of a complex with GPR87. 4. If the interface survives these checks, test binding at the bench (for example IL36RN-Fc binding to GPR87-expressing cells with and without PNGase F treatment, or SPR) and confirm GPR87 surface expression in keratinocytes or esophageal epithelium.

HCST ↔ GPR65 CONFIRMED_NOVEL VALIDATE low confidence ipTM 0.62 · pTM
Rationale
HCST (DAP10, UniProt Q9UBK5) and GPR65 (UniProt Q8IYL9) are both membrane proteins enhanced in the same tissues (bone marrow and lymphoid tissue) with overlapping vesicle/plasma-membrane compartments, and no database or literature records the pair (STRING not found, CellPhoneDB not found, PubMed co-occurrence 0). The supplied fold is moderately confident (ipTM 0.62, interface PAE 3.58 A, interface pLDDT 70.4) and looks partner-specific, since HCST passed the gate against only 1 of 11 receptors tested (p_twoway 0.003974). Confidence is low because I could not run a fold to check three open issues: a single-pass transmembrane helix packed against a 7-transmembrane bundle is a pose AF3 can produce without a lipid bilayer to constrain it; HCST's obligate partner KLRK1 (NKG2D) is absent from the model and may use the same helix face; and GPR65 has 3 N-glycosylation sites whose position relative to the interface is unknown.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt annotates GPR65 as a Glycoprotein with 3 Glycosylation features and 2 disulfide bonds; on a GPCR these are expected on the extracellular N-terminus and loops, but their position relative to the modeled interface is not known. UniProt annotates HCST with the keywords Glycoprotein and Phosphoprotein, but its feature counts show no Glycosylation feature and 1 Modified residue. The PubMed PTM search returned 0 hits for HCST and 6 hits for GPR65; the 5 titles returned are GPR65 physiology papers, not glycosylation studies. Neither protein's function text describes a glycan- or lipid-mediated binding mechanism. If the interface is transmembrane helix packing it would be largely unaffected by glycans, but this was not verified.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for HCST and GPR65 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for HCST and GPR65 (found: false). CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction (found: false), and GPR65 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. Nothing in any of the three sources indicates this pair is already known.
Other subunits
KLRK1
Follow-up (not yet executed): fold_complex was not available in this run, so the following are recommendations, not results. (1) Re-fold HCST + GPR65 and read interface_topology_a/b to confirm the contacts are transmembrane-to-transmembrane (optionally with same-side residues) and do not touch both Cytoplasmic and Extracellular residues at once; also check whether any of GPR65's 3 N-glycosylation sites sits on the interface. The supplied minimum separation of 1.77 A is tight and the 3D pose should be inspected for local clashes. (2) Fold HCST + KLRK1 + GPR65 (HCST is a disulfide-linked homodimer in the KLRK1-HCST receptor, so consider 2 copies of HCST) to test whether the GPR65 contact survives when HCST's obligate partner occupies its transmembrane face. CellPhoneDB covers only surface complexes, so a CORUM/STRING check for further partners is still needed. (3) Wet-lab: co-immunoprecipitation or proximity assay (BRET/NanoBiT) of GPR65 and DAP10 in NK or T cells, and test whether acidic pH activation of GPR65 alters DAP10 PI3-kinase/GRB2 signalling.

CATSPER2 ↔ GPR25 IMPLAUSIBLE low confidence ipTM 0.63 · pTM
Rationale
CATSPER2 (UniProt Q96P56) is a pore-forming subunit of the sperm-specific CatSper calcium channel in the sperm flagellum, whereas GPR25 (UniProt O00155) is the CXCL17 chemokine receptor acting on lymphocytes. Both are multi-pass integral membrane proteins, so a direct contact would require them to sit in the same membrane of the same cell, and HPA shows no tissue overlap (CATSPER2 enhanced in retina and testis; GPR25 enriched in stomach at only 1.8 nTPM). The AF3 interface (ipTM 0.63, interface pLDDT 75.3, 1 of 10 partner receptors confident, p_twoway 0.023) is moderately confident and appears partner-selective, but it is most likely transmembrane-helix packing between two polytopic proteins modelled without a bilayer and without CATSPER2's obligate channel partners. Confidence is low because HPA has no subcellular data for GPR25 and its expression signal is weak.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt lists no glycosylation or lipidation features for either protein; GPR25 has 1 disulfide bond, which is not a concern for the model. The PubMed PTM search returned 2 hits for CATSPER2 (a beta-defensin 20 docking study and the 2003 CatSper2 hyperactivated motility paper), neither of which is about a modification, and 0 hits for GPR25. There is no evidence for a PTM or glycan artifact; the concern is cell-type mismatch and missing channel subunits, not modifications.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for CATSPER2 and GPR25 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for CATSPER2 and GPR25. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction, and GPR25 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. The pair is unreported, but that novelty does not make it biologically plausible.
Other subunits
CATSPERB;CATSPERD;CATSPER1;CATSPER3;CATSPER4
Follow-up (not yet executed): No fold was run for this triage. If the pair is pursued, first inspect which residues form the modelled interface: contacts between the lipid-facing transmembrane helices of both proteins, or contacts mixing cytoplasmic and extracellular residues, would mark it as a bilayer-free artifact. Then re-fold CATSPER2 within its pore tetramer (CATSPER1, CATSPER3, CATSPER4; CellPhoneDB also lists the auxiliary subunits CATSPERB and CATSPERD) to test whether the GPR25-contacting surface is already occupied by the channel's own subunits. Before any binding assay, check for GPR25 transcript or protein in human spermatids and sperm using testis single-cell data, since without co-expression the interaction cannot occur.

FGA ↔ GPR39 LIKELY_SUBCOMPLEX low confidence ipTM 0.57 · pTM
Rationale
FGA is a secreted plasma protein that CellPhoneDB curates as an obligate member of the fibrinogen complex with FGB and FGG, so a fold of the FGA chain alone against GPR39 probably exposes coiled-coil surface that is buried in real fibrinogen. The pair is novel (STRING no hit, CellPhoneDB no hit, one unrelated PubMed co-mention) and topologically possible (blood-secreted ligand, plasma-membrane GPCR), but the structural support is weak: ipTM 0.57, interface pLDDT 55.7, minimum separation 1.67 A, and FGA passed the same interface gate against 2 of the 3 receptors it was tested with, so the signal is not specific to GPR39. GPR39 is a zinc-sensing receptor with no documented protein ligand, and both proteins carry glycosylation sites that the fold does not model.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt lists FGA (P02671) as a Glycoprotein and Phosphoprotein with 4 glycosylation sites, 16 modified residues and 8 disulfide bonds, including the inter-chain disulfides that tie it to FGB and FGG. UniProt lists GPR39 (O43194) as a Glycoprotein with 3 glycosylation sites, which on a GPCR are expected on the extracellular N-terminus and loops, the only face a secreted protein could contact. The PubMed PTM searches returned 22 hits for FGA and 25 for GPR39, but the top titles were not specific to glycosylation at any interface. Glycan interference at the modeled interface is a real but unverified caveat, because the interface residues could not be checked without a fold.
PubMed co-occurrence
One PubMed hit (PMID 37839873, Genes Genet Syst 2023), a methylation prognostic signature in lung squamous cell carcinoma; the two genes appear as independent signature members, unrelated to a physical interaction.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for FGA and GPR39. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction for the pair. PubMed co-occurrence returned 1 hit (PMID 37839873, a four-gene methylation signature for lymph node metastasis in lung squamous cell carcinoma), which is a co-mention in a gene signature and not evidence of binding. The pair is therefore not previously reported as an interaction.
Other subunits
FGB;FGG
Follow-up (not yet executed): Fold_complex was not available in this run. Recommended next steps: (1) re-fold GPR39 with FGA plus FGB and FGG (one fibrinogen half-molecule, or the relevant fragment) and see whether the FGA-GPR39 interface keeps a chain-pair ipTM of at least 0.5 once the FGA coiled-coil is paired with its native partners; (2) in that fold, check that the interface topology on GPR39 is extracellular/transmembrane only, with no cytoplasmic residues, and whether any of the 3 GPR39 or 4 FGA glycosylation sites lie on the interface; (3) inspect the 1.67 A minimum separation for steric clashes; (4) counter-screen FGA against additional unrelated GPCRs, since it already passed the gate against 2 of 3 receptors tested. A CORUM/STRING check of the fibrinogen complex membership would confirm the subcomplex call.

TDGF1 ↔ GPR31 CONFIRMED_NOVEL VALIDATE low confidence ipTM 0.58 · pTM
Rationale
TDGF1 (Cripto, UniProt P13385) is a secreted/GPI-anchored growth factor that can be shed to act on other cells, and GPR31 (UniProt O00270) is a plasma-membrane GPCR, so an extracellular ligand-receptor contact is topologically plausible. STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed (0 co-mentions) all return nothing for this pair, so it is novel. Confidence is low because the fold is only moderately confident (ipTM 0.58, interface pLDDT 66.4), both proteins carry one annotated extracellular glycosylation site whose position relative to the interface could not be checked without a fold, and GPR31's only documented ligands are a lipid (12-S-HETE) and protons, not a protein.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt annotates TDGF1 as a Glycoprotein and Lipoprotein with 1 Glycosylation feature, 1 Lipidation feature (the GPI anchor) and 6 disulfide bonds. UniProt annotates GPR31 as a Glycoprotein with 1 Glycosylation feature. The PubMed PTM searches returned 5 hits for TDGF1 and 2 for GPR31, but none of the titles specifically addresses glycosylation at a binding surface. Neither protein's function text describes a glycan-mediated binding mechanism. Because the modeled interface is large (buried surface area 2120 A^2, 309 contacts) and both glycosylation sites are on extracellular or secreted regions, a glycan at the interface cannot be excluded; this was not checked per residue because no fold was available in this run. It is recorded as an unresolved caveat, not as evidence of an artifact.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for TDGF1 and GPR31 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING found no association for TDGF1-GPR31. CellPhoneDB found no curated interaction, and GPR31 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. There is no hit in any of the three sources, which supports genuine novelty. The supplied breadth screen also argues for specificity: TDGF1 was confident against only 1 of 16 receptors tested (threshold interface PAE <= 3.75 A, no clash), with p_twoway = 0.008059.
Other subunits
ACVR1B;ACVR2A;ACVR2B;ACVR1C
Follow-up (not yet executed): 1. Re-fold TDGF1 with GPR31 and inspect the interface topology: confirm that the contacts fall on GPR31's extracellular N-terminus, extracellular loops or transmembrane-bundle mouth, and not on its cytoplasmic loops. An interface touching cytoplasmic residues would be physically impossible for a secreted or GPI-anchored ligand. 2. In the same fold, check whether the single annotated glycosylation site on TDGF1 or the one on GPR31, or the TDGF1 GPI-anchor attachment site, lies on the interface; if so, re-fold with the core glycan attached. 3. Test binding at the bench with soluble (shed) Cripto on GPR31-expressing cells, for example a binding assay or G-protein/ERK signalling readout with 12-S-HETE as the positive control. The CellPhoneDB Nodal receptor complexes listed under other_subunits (ACVR1B with ACVR2A or ACVR2B; ACVR1C with ACVR2B) are TDGF1's known partners and are given for context only; they are not proposed as missing subunits of a GPR31 complex.

IL17RE ↔ GPR153 INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE medium confidence ipTM 0.37 · pTM
Rationale
The pair is novel in every database checked (STRING not found, CellPhoneDB not found, 0 PubMed co-mentions), and both are plasma-membrane receptors per UniProt, so an in-membrane or ectodomain contact is not physically excluded. However, the AF3 ipTM of 0.37 is below the 0.5 convention for a predicted interaction, interface pLDDT is only 60.8, and HPA returned no subcellular localisation and no tissue or cell-type enrichment for either protein, so nothing independent supports the model. Two unmodelled factors further weaken it: IL17RE has 3 annotated N-glycosylation sites, and its curated co-receptor IL17RA (CellPhoneDB complex IL17_receptor_AE) was absent from the fold.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt annotates IL17RE as a Glycoprotein with 3 Glycosylation sites, which on a type I receptor would lie in the extracellular domain; AF3 did not model these glycans, and whether any site sits on the modelled interface is unknown. GPR153 has no annotated glycosylation or lipidation in UniProt. The PubMed PTM searches returned 4 hits for IL17RE and 1 for GPR153, but judging by their titles none is specifically about glycosylation or PTMs of these proteins (they concern IL-17C biology, Th17 subsets and GPR153 signalling).
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for IL17RE and GPR153 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for IL17RE and GPR153. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction, and GPR153 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. The pair is unreported, but that novelty is not backed by a confident fold.
Other subunits
IL17RA
Follow-up (not yet executed): No fold could be run in this triage. Recommended next steps: (1) re-fold IL17RE + GPR153 with IL17RA (and optionally the ligand IL17C) to see whether the interface survives or is displaced by the native co-receptor; (2) check the interface topology of the existing model to confirm it does not touch both cytoplasmic and extracellular residues at once, and whether any of the 3 IL17RE N-glycosylation sites lie on the interface; (3) if the interface involves the IL17RE ectodomain, re-fold with glycans attached. Note that the supplied breadth screen showed GPR153 confident against 1 of 5 receptors tested (p_twoway = 0.018981), which suggests some specificity, but with ipTM 0.37 this should not be treated as a positive signal without orthogonal evidence such as co-immunoprecipitation or a proximity assay in cells expressing both.

ADCYAP1 ↔ GPR142 INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE medium confidence ipTM 0.25 · pTM
Rationale
The pair is unreported (STRING not found, CellPhoneDB not found, 0 PubMed co-mentions) and topologically possible (secreted hormone plus a plasma-membrane GPCR), but nothing positively supports it. The supplied AF3 ipTM is 0.25, below the 0.5 convention for a predicted interaction, and the low interface PAE of 3.64 A that shortlisted it does not override that; the supplied two-way p-value of 0.057726 is also not below 0.05. Mechanistically, UniProt describes PACAP (P18509) as acting through the class B1 receptors VIPR1, VIPR2 and ADCYAP1R1, while GPR142 (Q7Z601) is a highly selective receptor for the aromatic amino acids L-Tryptophan and L-Phenylalanine, with no peptide-ligand precedent in the evidence pulled.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt lists no glycosylation or lipidation features for either protein. ADCYAP1 has 2 modified residues and the keywords Amidation and Cleavage on pair of basic residues: the bioactive forms are the proteolytically processed peptides PACAP27 and PACAP38, not the precursor. GPR142 has no PTM features annotated. The PubMed PTM search returned 6 hits for ADCYAP1, none of which concern glycosylation of PACAP itself among the titles returned, and 0 hits for GPR142. Glycan masking is not the concern here. The relevant caveat is precursor processing: the supplied complex size of 638 tokens suggests the full-length precursor was folded, though this was not confirmed, and the large buried surface area (5018 A^2, 1234 contacts) may involve propeptide regions that are absent from the secreted hormone.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 hits for ADCYAP1 together with GPR142.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for ADCYAP1 and GPR142. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction, and GPR142 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. The pair is therefore novel in the literature and databases, but novelty here is not accompanied by positive evidence of binding.
Follow-up (not yet executed): No fold could be run in this triage. The recommended next step is to re-fold GPR142 (Q7Z601) with the mature peptides PACAP38 and PACAP27 only, with C-terminal amidation if it can be represented, rather than the full precursor. Then check whether chain-pair ipTM reaches 0.5 and whether the contacts fall on the receptor's extracellular and transmembrane side rather than on cytoplasmic residues. As a positive control, fold the same peptide with ADCYAP1R1. The supplied screen data show the partner was confident, by the PAE gate, at 1 of 7 receptors tested; that specificity signal is only worth pursuing if the mature-peptide fold holds up. If it does, test it with a GPR142 Gq/IP1 or calcium assay using PACAP38, with L-Tryptophan as the reference agonist.

LRRTM2 ↔ GPR22 INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE low confidence ipTM 0.45 · pTM
Rationale
The pair is biologically plausible and has no prior report: both are cell-surface membrane glycoproteins per UniProt, both are expressed in brain, and STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed all return nothing for the pair. However, the supplied AF3 ipTM is 0.45, below the 0.5 convention for a predicted interaction, with a modest interface pLDDT of 60.2; the interaction PAE of 3.68 A only narrowly passes the 3.75 A shortlist gate. Both ectodomains carry N-glycosylation sites (4 on LRRTM2, 2 on GPR22) whose position relative to the modeled interface could not be checked because no fold tool was available in this run, so the evidence supports neither a confident novel call nor a confident artifact call.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt keywords mark both proteins as glycoproteins, with 4 annotated glycosylation sites on LRRTM2 and 2 on GPR22. The PubMed PTM search returned 0 hits for LRRTM2 and 2 hits for GPR22, neither of which concerns GPR22 glycosylation (one is on cardiomyocyte GPR22 overexpression, one on retroviral envelope glycosylation). The supplied interface is large (buried surface area 3229 A^2, 587 contacts), so glycans that AF3 did not model could plausibly overlap it, but whether any annotated site actually sits on the interface is unverified. Neither protein's function text describes a glycan-mediated binding mechanism.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for LRRTM2 and GPR22 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING found no association between LRRTM2 and GPR22. CellPhoneDB found no curated interaction, and GPR22 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table at all, so that miss is uninformative for GPR22. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. The pair is unreported. UniProt records neurexins (NRXN1-A and NRXN1-B) as the known presynaptic receptors for LRRTM2, by similarity; GPR22 is an orphan receptor that seems to act through a G(i)/G(o) pathway.
Other subunits
NRXN1
Follow-up (not yet executed): Not run here because fold tools were unavailable: (1) Re-fold LRRTM2 with GPR22 and read the interface topology, to confirm the contact is between the two extracellular regions and does not mix cytoplasmic and extracellular residues. (2) In the same fold, check whether any of the 4 LRRTM2 or 2 GPR22 N-glycosylation sites lie on the interface; if so, re-fold with the core glycan attached. (3) Consider a fold including NRXN1, the known LRRTM2 partner, to see whether GPR22 competes for or is compatible with the neurexin-binding face. The supplied breadth data (confident at 1 of 12 receptors tested, p_twoway=0.031759) suggests the signal is selective, so a wet-lab test such as a cell-surface binding assay or co-immunoprecipitation in neurons is reasonable only if the re-fold reaches a chain-pair ipTM of at least 0.5. The supplied minimum separation of 2.04 A should also be checked for local clashes.

COL26A1 ↔ GPR149 INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE medium confidence ipTM 0.24 · pTM
Rationale
The pair is unreported (STRING not found, CellPhoneDB not found, 0 PubMed co-mentions) and topologically plausible as a secreted extracellular-matrix collagen (COL26A1) meeting a plasma-membrane orphan GPCR (GPR149), with both genes brain-enhanced at the RNA level. However, the structural support does not reach the level of a predicted interaction: ipTM is 0.24 (below the 0.5 convention), interface pLDDT is 44.5, and the interface PAE of 3.69 A only just passes the 3.75 A gate, with a two-way p of 0.054523. Both proteins are also UniProt glycoproteins (2 and 3 annotated glycosylation sites) and COL26A1 was modelled without its collagen trimer partners, so the evidence neither supports a novel-interaction call nor shows the pair to be impossible.
PTM / glycosylation
UniProt marks both proteins with the Glycoprotein keyword: COL26A1 (Q96A83) has 2 annotated glycosylation sites and 3 disulfide bonds, plus the Hydroxylation keyword typical of collagens; GPR149 (Q86SP6) has 3 annotated glycosylation sites and 1 disulfide bond. The PubMed PTM/glycosylation search returned 0 hits for each gene. GPCR glycosylation sites are normally on the extracellular face, which is where a secreted collagen would have to bind, so glycan interference at the modelled surface is plausible, but whether any site lies on the modelled interface is not known because no per-residue interface analysis was available.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence for COL26A1 and GPR149 returned 0 hits.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING returned no association for COL26A1 and GPR149. CellPhoneDB returned no curated interaction, and GPR149 is not in the CellPhoneDB gene table. PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 papers. The pair is unreported in all three sources. GPR149 is annotated in UniProt as an orphan receptor.
Other subunits
COL26A1 (collagen homotrimer; additional copies of the same chain)
Follow-up (not yet executed): fold_complex was not available in this run. Recommended next steps: (1) re-fold GPR149 with three copies of COL26A1, since collagens function as triple-helical trimers and a single chain may present a non-native surface; CellPhoneDB lists no complex for either gene, so the trimer assumption comes from the collagen family, not from a curated complex entry. (2) In that fold, read the interface topology on GPR149 to confirm the contacts are on Extracellular residues and not Cytoplasmic ones, and check whether any of the 3 GPR149 or 2 COL26A1 glycosylation sites sits on the interface; if one does, re-fold with the glycan attached. (3) Inspect the supplied minimum separation of 1.91 A for local clashes, given the large reported interface (buried surface area 4485 A^2, 820 contacts) at an interface pLDDT of only 44.5, a combination that can indicate a low-confidence, extended chain draped over the receptor. Only 1 of 5 receptors tested against this partner passed the PAE gate, so the signal is not obviously promiscuous, but it should not be advanced to the bench unless a re-fold reaches a chain-pair ipTM of at least 0.5.

GRIN2A ↔ GPR12 INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE low confidence ipTM 0.57 · pTM
Rationale
The lectin flag does not hold up. As far as I recall, GPR12 is an orphan class-A GPCR whose UniProt function text describes constitutive Gs signaling and putative lipid ligands, not binding to beta-galactoside, sialic acid or any other glycan. It is not a galectin or siglec, so I am not moving to LIKELY_ARTIFACT_PTM. The glycosylation caveat stays as before: GRIN2A has extracellular glycosylation sites and GPR12 has 2, but neither protein binds partners through glycans. I could not re-read the UniProt text here, so this rests on recall. The fold status "topology_violation" is a real problem for the original LIKELY_SUBCOMPLEX call. It means the modelled contact is probably not physically possible for two membrane proteins, so the 4655 Å2 interface (ipTM 0.57, interface pLDDT 61.5) cannot be trusted as a "subcomplex" interface. The remaining evidence is weak: STRING, CellPhoneDB and PubMed all show nothing, and HPA plausibility is unknown. The pair is also not shown to be impossible, because both proteins are brain-enriched. I am downgrading to INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE.
PTM / glycosylation
GRIN2A has 7 glycosylation sites in its extracellular domains and GPR12 has 2. Neither protein is a lectin, so the glycan-mediated-binding concern does not apply to this pair. Unmodelled glycans are only a generic caveat.
PubMed co-occurrence
PubMed co-occurrence returned 0 hits for GRIN2A together with GPR12.
STRING / CellPhoneDB
STRING and CellPhoneDB returned no association for GRIN2A and GPR12, and PubMed co-occurrence found 0 papers. The pair is unreported (known_interaction_strength=none).
Other subunits
GRIN1
Follow-up (not yet executed): Re-fold GRIN2A with GRIN1 and a second GRIN2A copy, with GPR12 as the extra chain, and check whether the contact respects membrane topology. Check for co-expression in specific neuron types. Without a topology-consistent pose, treat the pair as unsupported.
